# Requirements and the tests that verify them

Generated by `tools/requirements_index.py` from the docstrings of `tests/`. Do not edit this file: run `python3 tools/requirements_index.py` after a test is added, removed or reworded (`--check` exits 1 when the file is stale; a test compares the two, REQ-DOC-004).

Every test states the requirement it verifies in its docstring, `/* REQ-<AREA>-<n>: <behaviour> */`. Below, one table per area in the order of the area names; an id belongs to one test, and the tool refuses an id that two tests carry. To run one test: `python -m unittest tests.<file>.<Class>.<method>` with the requirements of AGENTS.md, section 2.

1172 tests carry a requirement id, each its own, in 46 areas.

Every test carries a requirement id.

## ARCH

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-ARCH-001 | a folder name matches a style word only as a whole normalised segment: use-cases, UseCases and use_cases are one name; s3, class3, utils3d and api-gateways-old match nothing. | `tests/test_architecture.py::WordMatchingTest::test_names_match_whole_normalised_segments_only` |
| REQ-ARCH-002 | a long folder name one edit away from a style word matches it as a spelling variant; short names do not. | `tests/test_architecture.py::WordMatchingTest::test_spelling_variants_match_by_levenshtein` |
| REQ-ARCH-003 | persistence -> infra, usecases and interactor -> application, presenter -> interface adapters, viewmodel -> ui. | `tests/test_architecture.py::WordMatchingTest::test_architecture_synonyms_map_to_their_layer` |
| REQ-ARCH-004 | words with no architecture meaning match no layer of any style. | `tests/test_architecture.py::WordMatchingTest::test_unrelated_words_stay_unmatched` |
| REQ-ARCH-005 | matching the same names twice gives the same roles, steps and similarities. | `tests/test_architecture.py::WordMatchingTest::test_matching_is_deterministic` |
| REQ-ARCH-006 | a part starts at a folder with a manifest and ends at a nested part; the root is a part; a pom.xml below another is the same application. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_parts_start_at_manifests_and_small_parts_are_not_judged` |
| REQ-ARCH-007 | a part with routes, services, models and repositories folders is layered; the folders map to api, service, domain and infra. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_layered_backend_is_detected_and_its_folders_mapped` |
| REQ-ARCH-008 | below the routing root of a route-tree frontend, folders such as gateways are URL segments: the files are route or feature code, never infrastructure. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_a_route_tree_frontend_never_fires_backend_folder_rules` |
| REQ-ARCH-009 | a package with src/plugin.ts and commands, controllers, menu, views folders is a microkernel plug-in: no folder becomes a backend layer and no rule applies. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_a_plugin_package_is_microkernel_and_its_folders_are_not_layers` |
| REQ-ARCH-010 | a part whose folders support no style (a library) has style none: no layers, no rules. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_weak_evidence_leaves_the_part_without_layers` |
| REQ-ARCH-011 | a layer list of plain data is read lowest first; a pinned file belongs to the layer that pins it. | `tests/test_architecture.py::DeclaredRulesTest::test_a_dependency_cruiser_layer_table_is_read_in_order_with_pinned_files` |
| REQ-ARCH-012 | a layer entry that is not plain data (a spread) makes the file "not read". | `tests/test_architecture.py::DeclaredRulesTest::test_a_layer_table_with_computed_entries_is_not_read` |
| REQ-ARCH-013 | a part that declares its layers is labelled by them and forbids imports from a lower to a higher layer. | `tests/test_architecture.py::DeclaredRulesTest::test_a_part_with_its_own_rules_is_judged_by_them` |
| REQ-ARCH-014 | .wingbox/config.yaml written by init reads back to the same parts, styles and folder mapping. | `tests/test_architecture.py::ConfigTest::test_the_written_config_reads_back_to_the_same_layers` |
| REQ-ARCH-015 | the config names only known styles and layers of the chosen style; anything else is a typed error naming the part. | `tests/test_architecture.py::ConfigTest::test_an_unknown_style_or_layer_is_an_error` |
| REQ-ARCH-016 | init facts name style, score, source and evidence; the text prints the best style, its folders with counts and the other candidates. | `tests/test_architecture.py::InitOutputTest::test_the_text_is_a_printer_over_the_s_expressions` |
| REQ-ARCH-017 | main.ts, app.module.ts and Program.cs are composition roots: they keep their layer and are marked, so that the boundary rules let them import every layer. | `tests/test_architecture.py::CompositionRootTest::test_a_composition_root_is_labelled_but_marked` |
| REQ-ARCH-018 | a file named x.test.ts, x.spec.ts, x_test.go, test_x.py, x.test-helpers.ts or x.test-data.ts, or in a tests, \_\_tests\_\_, \_\_mocks\_\_, fixtures or e2e folder, is a test file. | `tests/test_architecture.py::TestFileTest::test_test_helpers_named_after_tests_are_tests` |
| REQ-ARCH-019 | without its own CQRS evidence (commands with queries and handlers) a part does not read \*.command.ts or commands/ as CQRS commands of the domain; with it, it does. | `tests/test_architecture.py::CqrsAndEntryPointsTest::test_command_files_are_cqrs_commands_only_when_the_part_has_the_cqrs_overlay` |
| REQ-ARCH-020 | a file that imports NestFactory from @nestjs/core, or Command or CommandRunner from nest-commander, starts the application: it is a composition root whatever its name. | `tests/test_architecture.py::CqrsAndEntryPointsTest::test_a_file_that_starts_the_application_is_a_composition_root` |
| REQ-ARCH-021 | in a route-tree frontend, a private folder at the top of the routing root (app/\_parts) is shared by the routes (ui), a deeper one belongs to its route (feature); a query, mutation, store, context or use-\* module outside the root is state; a utility that imports a component or state is a candidate, not a violation. | `tests/test_architecture.py::PartsAndDetectionTest::test_route_tree_reads_app_wide_private_folders_and_state_modules` |
| REQ-ARCH-023 | the target layout of a style lists every layer folder and every domain building block (Evans 2003, Vernon 2013); folders the part has are present with their real path, the others proposed where the style puts them. | `tests/test_architecture.py::TargetLayoutTest::test_the_target_layout_lists_present_and_proposed_folders_with_the_domain_blocks` |
| REQ-ARCH-024 | in the hexagonal target the repository interfaces are out ports of the application (application/ports/out), not a domain folder. | `tests/test_architecture.py::TargetLayoutTest::test_hexagonal_puts_repository_interfaces_in_the_out_ports` |
| REQ-ARCH-025 | advice is deterministic and of three kinds: move (a file its dependents import against the rules, with the counts), fix-violation (with the fix the style prescribes) and missing-folder (from the target layout); each names style and rule and has a stable id. | `tests/test_architecture.py::AdviceTest::test_each_kind_of_advice_names_style_rule_and_evidence` |
| REQ-ARCH-026 | a part whose style is not determined gets no advice, and says so. | `tests/test_architecture.py::AdviceTest::test_a_part_without_a_determined_style_gets_no_advice` |
| REQ-ARCH-027 | the store logs advice once with a stable id; on the next run a created folder is followed, a file moved to the advised folder is followed, a file moved elsewhere is done differently and teaches that folder's layer, advice still open for 3 builds of a changed graph is hidden (logged once). | `tests/test_architecture.py::DecisionsStoreTest::test_outcomes_are_read_from_the_next_run` |
| REQ-ARCH-028 | `advice reject` hides the item and ranks advice of the same kind and rule in the part lower; an unknown id is refused. | `tests/test_architecture.py::DecisionsStoreTest::test_a_rejected_item_is_not_shown_and_lowers_its_rule` |
| REQ-ARCH-029 | align gives a part the share of placed files, of dependencies that follow the rules, the layers present and missing, the folders without a layer and the broken rules with a count and an example. | `tests/test_align.py::AlignTest::test_align_reports_placed_share_rules_followed_and_gaps` |
| REQ-ARCH-030 | learning takes the closest style of the library as the base; a folder it does not place is derived by its file names (\*.controller.ts), then by its name in another style (strategies is an api folder in nestjs), then by its imports; folders the style places are never relabelled, and what still breaks a rule is the baseline. | `tests/test_align.py::AlignTest::test_learning_keeps_the_closest_style_and_derives_the_rest_by_names_patterns_imports` |
| REQ-ARCH-031 | a request is placed by the [[placement]] entry of the part's style whose words it shares most (whole words, plural folded), with the layers that layer may import; unrelated words place nothing. | `tests/test_place.py::PlaceTest::test_the_words_of_the_request_choose_the_placement_of_the_style` |
| REQ-ARCH-032 | an accepted item stays shown: later builds, each of a changed graph, do not count it as ignored. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::DecisionsStateTest::test_accepted_advice_is_never_counted_as_ignored` |
| REQ-ARCH-033 | a build logs one run line, new advice and changed states; an open item adds nothing per build until it becomes hidden after 3 ignored builds of a changed graph, which is logged once. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::DecisionsStateTest::test_the_store_logs_changes_of_state_not_every_run` |
| REQ-ARCH-034 | a folder whose name starts with the word test, tests, spec or e2e (tests-integration, test-utils) is a test folder, a feature named after tests (openappa-policy-tests) is not; a shared or common folder inside a layer folder belongs to that layer (services/shared is service), a utils folder stays a utility. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::LabelsTest::test_test_folder_words_and_shared_inside_a_layer` |
| REQ-ARCH-035 | when a layer has learned or configured folders besides the style's own (controllers), the target and place name the style's own folder first. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::TargetAndLearningTest::test_a_learned_folder_never_displaces_the_style_folder_of_a_layer` |
| REQ-ARCH-036 | learned layers of the decisions store apply only when there is no .wingbox/config.yaml; with a config file, the config states the layers. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::TargetAndLearningTest::test_the_config_wins_over_learned_layers` |
| REQ-ARCH-037 | the domain building blocks are proposed only for a style with a domain layer and a part that has domain code; in Python and Rust their folders are snake_case. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::DomainBlocksTest::test_domain_blocks_only_with_a_domain_layer_and_domain_code` |
| REQ-ARCH-038 | a pair whose import is for types only is a violation-type-only, even when a data-flow edge joins the same files; it is never also a candidate or a violation. | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::TypeOnlyPairTest::test_a_pair_imported_for_types_only_is_listed_once` |
| REQ-ARCH-039 | align and its baseline use the rule set of `boundaries`: a pair imported for types only and a pair whose source is a composition root (a module without a rule-based layer) are not breaks; a candidate rule gives no break. | `tests/test_align.py::AlignTest::test_align_and_boundaries_count_the_same_breaks` |
| REQ-ARCH-040 | besides the placement texts, a layer's name, folder words and file-name patterns count (plural -ies folded, a word may be the start of a folder word: utility/utils): NestJS repository class, DTO, domain entity, notification service, background job; hexagonal domain entity and REST endpoint; route-tree utility and shared React component. A placement without may_import says not stated. | `tests/test_place.py::PlaceTest::test_layer_words_and_file_patterns_count_and_ties_go_to_the_richer_match` |
| REQ-ARCH-041 | an import and a data-flow edge of one pair give one advice item; a layer file without a layer order is "not read: it states no layer order". | `tests/test_review_fixes.py::AdviceAndWordingTest::test_one_advice_item_per_pair_and_a_readable_not_read_reason` |
| REQ-ARCH-042 | `wingbox init --yes` on a repository with 1, 3 or 11 source files (too few to judge) writes a config.yaml that `wingbox build` accepts: the build exits 0 and writes the graph; a repository with no source file is a new project, which init sets up with --language and --style, and that config is accepted too. | `tests/test_small_repository.py::SmallRepositoryTest::test_init_on_a_small_repository_writes_a_config_that_build_accepts` |
| REQ-ARCH-043 | a config.yaml whose `parts` is empty or missing, as 0.6.0 to 0.8.0 wrote for a small repository, is read as no parts declared: the whole repository is one undetermined part, the build writes the graph and says so in one line. | `tests/test_small_repository.py::SmallRepositoryTest::test_a_config_without_parts_from_an_earlier_version_is_read_as_no_parts_declared` |
| REQ-ARCH-044 | a config.yaml broken in another way (`parts` that is not a mapping, a part with an unknown style) is still an error, and the message names the line of the file. | `tests/test_small_repository.py::SmallRepositoryTest::test_a_config_broken_by_hand_names_the_line` |
| REQ-ARCH-045 | after `init --yes` and `build` on a repository too small to judge, learn, align, advice, place, compare, boundaries and the search hook answer without a traceback; boundaries says the part was not checked. | `tests/test_small_repository.py::SmallRepositoryTest::test_every_command_works_on_a_small_repository` |
| REQ-ARCH-046 | a build whose graph hash equals the one of the run before does not count as a run in which open advice was ignored: six builds of the same graph hide nothing, and the count goes on with the next graph that differs. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::IgnoredRunsTest::test_a_rebuild_of_the_same_graph_is_not_an_ignored_run` |
| REQ-ARCH-047 | advice of kind fix-violation, fix-capability or fix-pattern is not marked ignored and stays shown however many builds of changed code pass, while a layout suggestion (missing-folder) of the same store is hidden after 3. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::IgnoredRunsTest::test_advice_to_fix_a_finding_is_never_hidden_by_inaction` |
| REQ-ARCH-048 | a decisions store that already holds an ignored outcome for fix-violation advice shows the item again; a rejection with a reason is what hides it, and needs_reason is true for fix advice only. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::IgnoredRunsTest::test_a_fix_hidden_by_an_older_version_is_shown_again` |
| REQ-ARCH-049 | read_baseline_terms returns owner, reason and end date of the entries that state any of them, for baseline and baseline-capability lines; terms_sexp writes exactly the stated fields; an entry is expired from the day after its end date and when the date cannot be read. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::BaselineTermsUnitTest::test_terms_are_read_and_written_back` |

| REQ-ARCH-051 | a baseline entry whose :expires date has passed, or cannot be read as a date, no longer hides its break: boundaries prints it as violation with :expired and the owner, counts it, exits 1, and `wingbox advice` asks to fix it again. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::BaselineTermsCommandTest::test_an_entry_past_its_end_date_is_a_violation_again` |
| REQ-ARCH-052 | a baseline entry that states no owner, reason or end date gives the same violation-known line and the same arch-known fact as before the terms existed: nothing is added. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::BaselineTermsCommandTest::test_an_entry_without_terms_prints_as_before` |
| REQ-ARCH-053 | `wingbox align --learn` writes new entries without terms, as before, and keeps the owner, the reason and the end date of an entry that states them; an entry past its end date stays past it, so learning again does not accept it again. | `tests/test_baseline_terms.py::BaselineTermsCommandTest::test_align_learn_keeps_the_terms_a_person_wrote` |

| REQ-ARCH-057 | a part of `.wingbox/config.yaml` with `style: declared` may state `order:`, a list of `<layer>: [<folder>, ...]` entries, lowest layer first, the folders relative to the part (`.` for the files directly in it). A file is in the layer whose folder holds it; a file may depend on its own layer and on the layers below, so an import of a higher layer is a boundary violation with the rule `"<from> -> <to>"`; test files are in no layer. `wingbox layers` names the config file as the rule. An `order` that is no list of such entries, or that names a folder twice, stops the build with a message that names `order`. | `tests/test_layers_from_folders.py::OrderedLayersTest::test_a_part_states_its_layers_in_order` |
| REQ-ARCH-058 | `wingbox init --from-folders <part>` (`.` for the repository) reads the graph, updates the project .gitignore and writes that part into `.wingbox/config.yaml` with `style: declared` and an `order:` of one layer per folder of the part, lowest first: a folder stands below the folders that depend on it; folders that depend on each other are ordered so that the fewest imports go upward. It prints the order and the imports that go upward under it, by pair of folders (`--sexp`: `(layers-from-folders ...)`, `(layer-order ...)`, `(upward ...)`, `(arch-config-written ...)`). A part that has layer rules already is kept unless `--force`; without a graph, and for a part with fewer than two folders, nothing is written and the answer says why. | `tests/test_layers_from_folders.py::OrderedLayersTest::test_init_writes_the_layers_from_the_order_of_the_folders` |
| REQ-ARCH-059 | after `wingbox init --from-folders` and a build, `wingbox boundaries` reports the imports that go upward under the written order as violations; `wingbox align --learn` records them in `.wingbox/baseline.sexp`, after which they are counted as known and a new upward import is the only violation. | `tests/test_layers_from_folders.py::OrderedLayersTest::test_the_written_layers_are_checked_and_todays_breaks_can_be_accepted` |
| REQ-ARCH-060 | C and C++ files are source files of a part: `wingbox init --from-folders <folder>` on a folder of C++ code that is no part yet adds it as a part, the build keeps the part, its files get the layers of the order and an include of a higher layer is a violation. | REQ-BCOV-016 | with --strict, a violation in a checked part beside a part that was not checked gives :status fail and the exit status 1, and the result line still carries the number of files not checked. | `tests/test_boundaries_strict.py::BoundariesStrictTest::test_a_violation_outranks_a_part_not_checked` |
| REQ-BCOV-017 | with --strict, a scope that matches no module gives the no-match error, then boundaries-result :status incomplete :reason no-files-checked, and the exit status 2. | `tests/test_boundaries_strict.py::BoundariesStrictTest::test_a_scope_that_matches_nothing_is_incomplete` |
| REQ-BCOV-018 | with --strict, a break listed in .wingbox/baseline.sexp is counted in :known and not in :violations; the result is pass and the exit status 0. | `tests/test_boundaries_strict.py::BoundariesStrictTest::test_a_break_accepted_in_the_baseline_is_counted_as_known` |
| REQ-BCOV-019 | with --strict, source files that belong to no part of the configured architecture are listed as boundaries-not-checked with the reason in-no-part and make the result incomplete (exit status 2); plain mode lists them the same way, with boundaries-partial in place of ok, and exits 0. | `tests/test_boundaries_strict.py::BoundariesStrictTest::test_files_in_no_part_are_not_checked` |
| REQ-BCOV-020 | the source files of a part that are written in a language no parser reads are in no module, so no rule judged them: the graph states their number per part and language (arch-not-parsed), boundaries lists them as boundaries-not-checked with the reason not-parsed-language and the languages, prints boundaries-partial instead of ok, and with --strict the result is incomplete with the reason not-parsed-language and the exit status 2. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::FilesNotReadTest::test_files_of_a_language_without_a_parser_are_not_checked` |
| REQ-BCOV-021 | a part whose every source file is in a language no parser reads has no module in the graph and is still reported: boundaries all --strict names it as not checked and does not pass on the other part alone. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::FilesNotReadTest::test_a_part_without_any_module_is_not_a_pass` |
| REQ-BCOV-022 | a shell script, a Dockerfile, a view template and a file of an unparsed language in a test folder or in an ignored folder are not source files of a part: they are not counted as not read, and the gate passes as before. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::FilesNotReadTest::test_files_the_parts_do_not_count_as_source_change_nothing` |
| REQ-BCOV-023 | with --strict, a graph that is known to miss a changed file (the refresh is off or failed) is not a pass: the result is incomplete with the reason graph-stale and the exit status 2, and no boundaries-ok line is printed; without --strict the answer stays as it was, with the warning on standard error. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_graph_known_to_be_stale_is_incomplete` |
| REQ-BCOV-024 | with the refresh on, the same change is read before the answer: the gate fails on the violation and names no stale graph. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_refreshed_graph_passes_or_fails_on_its_facts` |
| REQ-BCOV-025 | the graph records what the build read of .wingbox/config.yaml, baseline.sexp and the learned layers of decisions.sexp; when one of them was edited, added or deleted since, boundaries names the files in a boundaries-rules-changed fact and prints no ok, with --strict the result is incomplete with the reason rules-changed (exit status 2), and after a build the gate answers from the new facts. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_rule_file_changed_after_the_build_is_noticed` |
| REQ-BCOV-026 | of .wingbox/decisions.sexp only the learned layers are rules, and only without a config: the lines a build logs (build-run, advice-given, advice-outcome) leave the recorded digest as it is, a learned-layer line changes it, and with a config.yaml the learned layers are not applied and do not count. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_the_log_lines_of_a_build_are_not_a_change_of_the_rules` |
| REQ-BCOV-027 | a learned-layer line added to .wingbox/decisions.sexp after the build makes boundaries name that file as changed, and --strict incomplete with the reason rules-changed; the log lines the build wrote itself did not. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_learned_layer_added_after_the_build_is_noticed` |
| REQ-BCOV-028 | without --strict, source files that belong to no part of the configured architecture are listed as boundaries-not-checked with the reason in-no-part and boundaries-partial replaces ok, with the same counts as --strict gives; the exit status stays 0. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_plain_mode_does_not_say_ok_for_files_in_no_part` |
| REQ-BCOV-029 | with a violation in the checked part and files in no part, boundaries-summary counts the files that were checked (not every file of the scope), and boundaries-partial follows it without --strict as with it. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_a_violation_beside_files_in_no_part_is_counted_the_same_in_both_modes` |
| REQ-BCOV-030 | with --strict, boundaries-ok is printed only in an answer whose result is pass: a project without a file to check ends in the incomplete result alone. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_strict_mode_prints_no_ok_line_unless_the_result_is_pass` |
| REQ-BCOV-031 | when the layer rules hold and a file does what its layer may not, boundaries prints the capability violation and its summary without a boundaries-ok line before them, with and without --strict; the exit status is 1. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_no_ok_line_before_capability_violations` |
| REQ-BCOV-032 | a file of a checked part that no layer rule places is not judged and is not counted in :checked: boundaries states the number per part in a boundaries-no-layer fact and as :no-layer on its ok, summary and result lines (the keyword is absent when the number is zero), and the gate still passes, because the rules say nothing about such files. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_files_no_rule_places_are_not_counted_as_checked` |
| REQ-BCOV-033 | a part that has rules but no file in any of its layers was not checked: boundaries lists it as boundaries-not-checked with the reason no-file-in-a-layer and prints boundaries-partial; with --strict the result is incomplete with the reason no-files-checked and the exit status 2. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_a_part_in_which_no_file_is_placed_is_not_checked` |
| REQ-BCOV-034 | without --strict, boundaries for a scope that matches no module prints the no-match error and exits 2 (nothing was checked), as --strict does. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CoverageCountsTest::test_a_scope_that_matches_nothing_is_not_a_success` |
| REQ-BCOV-035 | when the record of what changed since the build cannot be read and the refresh is off, the graph is reported stale with no changed file counted; the gate is told so all the same and --strict is incomplete with the reason graph-stale. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_state_that_cannot_be_read_is_stale_for_the_gate` |
| REQ-BCOV-036 | when the refresh before the query fails (a config that cannot be read, written after the build, and a changed source file), the graph is stale with the reason refresh-failed: --strict is incomplete with the reason graph-stale and the exit status 2 although the old facts hold no violation, and the changed config is named. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::StaleFactsTest::test_a_refresh_that_failed_leaves_the_gate_incomplete` |

## BENCH

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-BENCH-001 | benchmarks/impact/README.md must equal what make_report.py writes from benchmarks/impact/results. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_report_is_the_output_of_the_report_script_on_the_committed_results` |
| REQ-BENCH-002 | benchmark results, reference lists, logs and scripts must not contain absolute paths of a local machine. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_committed_benchmark_files_name_no_path_of_the_machine_that_ran_them` |
| REQ-BENCH-003 | the build-time summary must give, per corpus, the median wall time of the build and the size of the graph it wrote. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_build_time_summary_takes_the_median_of_the_rounds` |
| REQ-BENCH-004 | the build-time summary must stop when two builds of one corpus wrote graphs of different size. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_build_time_summary_refuses_rounds_that_built_different_graphs` |
| REQ-BENCH-005 | the interval of "k of n" must be the 95 % Wilson score interval, which stays inside 0..1 and is wide for small n. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_interval_of_a_proportion_is_the_wilson_interval` |
| REQ-BENCH-006 | a named file that the reference accepts must not count as outside the list, and a missing accepted file must not count as missing. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_an_accepted_file_is_neither_required_nor_wrong` |
| REQ-BENCH-007 | for a Rust path crate::a::b the file of module a must be accepted for the file that wrote the path, and not required. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_a_rust_path_accepts_the_modules_it_passes_through` |
| REQ-BENCH-008 | benchmarks/graphify/README.md must equal what its tools/make_report.py writes from benchmarks/graphify/results. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_comparison_report_is_the_output_of_its_report_script` |
| REQ-BENCH-009 | benchmarks/php/README.md must equal what its make_report.py writes from benchmarks/php/results, and every committed result file must contain no absolute path of a local machine. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_php_report_and_results_name_no_local_path` |
| REQ-BENCH-010 | the same-machine build summary of the three tools must give the median wall time of every step, cold and after an edit, and stop when two cold runs, or two runs after the edit, of one tool wrote graphs of different size. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_same_machine_build_summary_takes_medians_and_refuses_different_graphs` |
| REQ-BENCH-011 | benchmarks/usages/README.md must equal what its make_report.py writes from benchmarks/usages/results. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_usages_report_is_the_output_of_its_report_script` |
| REQ-BENCH-012 | a use site counts as found when a tool names its file and line, whatever column it gives, so tools that print no column are scored on the same sites. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkReportTest::test_a_use_site_is_scored_by_file_and_line` |
| REQ-BENCH-013 | both copies of tools/py_edges.py count `importlib.import_module("a.b")`, `import_module("a.b")` and `__import__("a.b")` with a literal absolute name as an edge to that module (kind import-call), with the package \_\_init\_\_.py files on the way as implied pairs; a name built at run time, a relative name and a module outside the tree give no edge. | `tests/test_reference_py_edges.py::PyEdgesTest::test_a_module_loaded_by_a_literal_name_is_an_edge` |
| REQ-BENCH-014 | the impact benchmark draws its questions from the files that an import statement reaches, so that counting modules loaded by name changes no question; the edge of a module loaded by name is still in the reference list of every question. | `tests/test_reference_py_edges.py::PyEdgesTest::test_questions_are_drawn_from_import_statements_only` |
| REQ-BENCH-015 | both copies of tools/rust_resolve.py give, for a name used through a facade, the file that defines it (to_followed), skipping a private item a glob re-export cannot carry; a glob import (`use crate::domain::*`) gives a glob-use reference for each item its module text names, also as the head of a path (`Kind::A`), and none for the variant of a local enum or a typed local name; the impact benchmark requires both the facade and the definer, and still draws its questions from the facade edges. | `tests/test_reference_rust.py::RustReferenceTest::test_the_reference_follows_items_to_their_definers` |
| REQ-BENCH-016 | both copies of tools/rust_resolve.py give a type-member reference for a member named through a type path (`Type::member`, `Self::member`, `<Type>::member`, `Type::<T>::member`, the type named by a `use`, a path, a glob import or a type alias) that an inherent impl block defines in another file than the one that names it: to is the file of the type, to_followed the file of the impl block (the file of the type itself when it defines the member: a type named through an alias of a third module, `Handle::defaults()`, is reached only this way); none for a method called on a value or a trait impl; the impact benchmark requires the edge and does not draw its questions from it. | `tests/test_reference_rust.py::RustReferenceTest::test_the_reference_follows_a_type_path_to_the_file_of_the_impl_block` |
| REQ-BENCH-017 | tools/rust_compiler_check.py reads a SCIP index of rust-analyzer without any dependency, finds the file of each module and definition, and classifies each name that resolves into another file: a `mod` declaration, the last module of a path, an item in a `use`, a member of an inherent impl block named through a type path (`Store::open`, counted), a method called on a value (`.size()`, outside the question), a visibility path (`pub(super)`, outside); a required pair of the reference the index does not have is listed as not confirmed, a counted edge the reference lacks as missing in the reference. | `tests/test_rust_compiler_check.py::RustCompilerCheckTest::test_edges_of_an_index_are_classified_and_compared_with_the_reference` |
| REQ-BENCH-018 | both copies of tools/rust_resolve.py resolve a crate named by `[lib] name`, by a dependency renamed with `package = ".."`, by `extern crate a as b;` and `extern crate self as me;`, and by `::name` in code; find the binary of `src/bin/<name>/main.rs` and its modules; start a `use` path and `::a` at the crate root in a package of edition 2015 (inherited from the workspace); declare every file of `#[cfg_attr(cond, path = "..")]` and look the children of an inline module with `#[path = "dir"]` up in that folder; take a variant re-exported with `pub use kinds::Kind::*;` to the file of the enum; give no glob-use reference for a name the module imports by name; and carry a `pub(super)` or `pub(in path)` item of a glob re-exported module only into that scope. | `tests/test_reference_rust.py::RustReferenceTest::test_the_reference_follows_cargo_names_editions_and_module_attributes` |
| REQ-BENCH-019 | the 0.12 releases install the parsers of C#, Java, Go, Ruby, PHP and Dart only with the extra `all`, which later versions keep, so every benchmark script installs the Wingbox under test (and the one it compares with) with the extra `all`: a release spec becomes wingbox[all]==X.Y.Z, a checkout or a file its path with [all]. | `tests/test_benchmark_report.py::BenchmarkInstallTest::test_every_benchmark_installs_wingbox_with_all_language_parsers` |
| REQ-BENCH-020 | every call of a Wingbox query command in the benchmark scripts names the form it reads: --sexp for the flat facts, and for the text a published size was measured on the option text_form gives for the Wingbox under test (`--human` when its help names `--human\|--json`, nothing for an earlier release, whose default is that text). So a run parses and measures what it did before the default changed, with a pinned release as with this checkout: wingbox_files of benchmarks/impact and benchmarks/php finds the dependent files in `impact <file> 2 --all --human`, and the patterns of benchmarks/usages find the use sites in `usages <target> --sexp --all`. | `tests/test_output_formats_cli.py::BenchmarkReaderTest::test_the_benchmark_scripts_name_the_form_they_read` |
| REQ-BENCH-021 | the reference of the C and C++ benchmark takes every `#include` line outside a comment, inside an `#if` block too; a quoted name is the file beside the including file, then the file in an include root; a name in angle brackets is looked for in the include roots only; a name found nowhere is counted as external and gives no edge. | `tests/test_benchmark_cpp_collisions.py::CppReferenceTest::test_the_reference_reads_the_include_lines_as_a_compiler_looks_for_them` |
| REQ-BENCH-022 | the compiler check counts, per translation unit, the files of the tree `clang -MM` reads and how many of them the reference reaches; with one edge taken out of the reference the file it led to is reported as not reached. | `tests/test_benchmark_cpp_collisions.py::CppReferenceTest::test_the_compiler_confirms_the_reference_and_names_what_it_does_not_reach` |
| REQ-BENCH-023 | on the corpus of benchmarks/collisions (a package beside a module file of its name, a module named like its package, a folder without `__init__.py` beside a module file, a package under `src/`, a script beside a script, packages with the same last name), the import edges of the graph of this checkout are those the Python interpreter loads, statement by statement: none wrong, none missing; the reference holds at least 50 edges read from at least 60 loads. | `tests/test_benchmark_cpp_collisions.py::CollisionBenchmarkTest::test_the_graph_of_this_checkout_equals_what_the_interpreter_loads` |
| REQ-BENCH-024 | benchmarks/cpp/README.md is what `make_report.py` writes from `benchmarks/cpp/results`, and benchmarks/collisions/README.md what `run.py --report` writes from its results: no number of either page is typed by hand. | `tests/test_benchmark_cpp_collisions.py::BenchmarkPagesTest::test_the_pages_are_what_the_result_files_say` |
| REQ-BENCH-025 | the README says that `docker build -f Dockerfile.benchmark ...` and `docker run --rm wingbox-benchmark` compare every result with the committed one, so the Wingbox that benchmarks/impact/run.sh installs when WINGBOX_SPEC is not set is the release that benchmarks/impact/results/versions.json names, and Dockerfile.benchmark, whose entry point is that script, sets no other version. | `tests/test_review_wp9_mcp_ci_docs.py::BenchmarkDefaultTest::test_the_container_of_the_readme_runs_the_version_whose_results_are_committed` |

## CAP

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-CAP-001 | SQL passed to a query call, query-builder and ORM queries, database and HTTP clients, framework imports, HTTP responses and environment reads are found by syntax with their line, in Python, TypeScript, PHP, Ruby, Java, C# and Go; comment lines are skipped. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityDetectionTest::test_each_language_reports_the_line_and_the_evidence` |
| REQ-CAP-002 | a SQL statement in a string literal that no query call on the line (or the call opened just above) receives is a suspected finding; prose such as "select an option from the list" is no SQL; a TypeScript `import type` of a client is suspected too (the compiler erases it); the text of a multi-line string (a docstring, a script of another program in a template) is not code, only the SQL of a query call opened just above it counts; `db.delete('store', key)` of IndexedDB is no query builder. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityDetectionTest::test_a_sql_literal_not_passed_to_a_query_call_is_only_suspected` |
| REQ-CAP-003 | in a layered Python part, SQL in a repository passes; the same query in a service is a capability-violation naming the file, layer, allowed layers, line and evidence; a query kept in a variable is a candidate; the ORM and the framework in a domain file break (domain-imports-orm); the environment read in config passes; a controller that imports a repository with database access skips the service (a logging "repository" does not count); a service that builds a JSONResponse breaks its pattern. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_python_sql_in_a_repository_passes_and_elsewhere_breaks_with_the_line` |
| REQ-CAP-004 | in a layered TypeScript part, Prisma in a repository passes; db.query with SQL in a service and axios in a controller break with their lines; a template literal query in a variable is a candidate; a NestJS import in an entity breaks the domain rule. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_typescript_rules_follow_the_same_layers` |
| REQ-CAP-005 | `capabilities:` of a part in .wingbox/config.yaml replaces the style's rule for that capability; a `(baseline-capability <part> <module> <capability>)` line of .wingbox/baseline.sexp turns the violation into capability-violation-known. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_the_config_restates_a_rule_and_the_baseline_accepts_a_finding` |
| REQ-CAP-006 | REPORT.md has a "What layers do" section with each violating file, its capability or pattern, layer, line and evidence; `wingbox advice --sexp` lists fix-capability and fix-pattern items with the fix the style prescribes. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_report_and_advice_name_the_file_the_line_and_the_fix` |
| REQ-CAP-007 | in a layered PHP (Laravel) part, DB::select in a repository passes and the same call in a controller breaks with its line; an Http facade call in a service breaks the http-client rule. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_laravel_queries_belong_to_repositories` |
| REQ-CAP-008 | `wingbox align --learn` writes each capability violation into .wingbox/baseline.sexp as baseline-capability; after a rebuild they are capability-violation-known and only new ones are violations. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_align_learn_accepts_the_current_breaks_in_the_baseline` |
| REQ-CAP-009 | the findings are kept by content hash in .wingbox/cache.capabilities.json (ignored by .wingbox/.gitignore); a rebuild takes an unchanged file's findings from it and reads a changed file again. | `tests/test_capabilities.py::CapabilityRulesTest::test_a_rebuild_reads_again_only_changed_files` |
| REQ-CAP-010 | in a part with capability rules, the files of a language the capability detector does not read are stated: the graph holds their number per part and language (arch-capability-unread), and boundaries prints a capability-not-read fact with the part, the language and the number; the status does not change. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CapabilityDetectorTest::test_a_language_the_detector_does_not_read_is_named` |
| REQ-CAP-011 | a part whose files the capability detector reads (TypeScript) has no arch-capability-unread fact and boundaries prints no capability-not-read line. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CapabilityDetectorTest::test_languages_the_detector_reads_give_no_such_fact` |
| REQ-CAP-012 | the capability detector reads at most MAX_LINE_LENGTH characters of a line: a query call within them is found, one that starts after them is not, and a line of 60000 characters of words that start SQL statements without ending them costs no more than its first MAX_LINE_LENGTH characters. | `tests/test_review_wp3_gate.py::CapabilityDetectorTest::test_the_detector_reads_a_bounded_part_of_a_long_line` |

| REQ-CLI-010 | `wingbox init --sexp` prints the S-expression facts (arch-part, arch-evidence, arch-config-written, and last init-assistant-setup naming .wingbox/assistant-setup.md) for agents. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxInitTest::test_init_sexp_prints_the_facts_the_text_is_made_from` |
| REQ-CLI-011 | `wingbox build` writes .wingbox/index.sexp and .wingbox/cache.json (no file at the old names); every layer label and violation names the style and the rule, in the graph, `boundaries`, REPORT.md and the map. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxBuildArchitectureTest::test_build_writes_the_index_into_dot_wingbox_with_labels_that_name_style_and_rule` |
| REQ-CLI-012 | with .wingbox/config.yaml, build takes styles and folders from it (match config); a part marked undetermined gets no layers from folder rules and no boundary violations, and boundaries says it was not checked instead of ok. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxBuildArchitectureTest::test_build_follows_the_config_and_an_undetermined_part_has_no_violations` |
| REQ-CLI-013 | query commands find .wingbox/index.sexp in the current folder or a parent and resolve files against the folder above .wingbox/. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxBuildArchitectureTest::test_queries_find_the_graph_in_dot_wingbox_of_a_parent_folder` |
| REQ-CLI-014 | a part that declares its layers (dependency-cruiser layers.mjs) is judged by them: init names the file, an import from a lower to a higher layer is a violation of the declared order, and a pair listed in the project's known exceptions is a violation-known with :known. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxDeclaredRulesTest::test_a_part_with_its_own_layer_rules_is_judged_by_them_and_its_baseline_is_named` |
| REQ-CLI-015 | in a NestJS part, a CLI command (nest-commander) and a worker bootstrap that imports the app module break no rule, and a utility that imports a repository is a candidate with the reason "utility depends on infrastructure; move it to services or invert", not a violation. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxNestEntryPointsTest::test_entry_points_are_not_violations_and_a_utility_on_infrastructure_is_a_candidate` |

| REQ-CLI-019 | `wingbox align` prints the gaps of each part (placed files, rules followed, unplaced folders, broken rules), `--to` maps the layers onto another style, `--learn` writes the learned folders into .wingbox/config.yaml with a comment and the remaining violations into .wingbox/baseline.sexp, after which build and boundaries report only new violations. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxAlignTest::test_align_reports_gaps_learns_and_then_reports_only_new_violations` |
| REQ-CLI-020 | for a part with declared rules, `align --learn` guesses nothing: the baseline is the current breaks of the declared order, each marked with the project's own baseline file when it lists the pair, else align. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxAlignTest::test_align_learn_on_declared_rules_takes_the_project_baseline` |
| REQ-CLI-021 | `wingbox place "<what>"` names the layer of the part's style for the request, the folder (present, or new from the target layout) and what that layer may import; a request with no matching words is answered with place-none. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxPlaceTest::test_place_names_the_layer_and_the_folder_present_or_new` |
| REQ-CLI-022 | with .wingbox/config.yaml the config states the layers: a learned folder it does not list is not applied, and the build records the removal in the decisions store. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxDecisionsTest::test_removing_a_learned_folder_from_the_config_undoes_it` |
| REQ-CLI-023 | `place` in a part with declared rules (asked with --part or from inside it) names the folder whose words match the request and the project's own layer of that folder, with example files; a style whose where is prose gives it as :where, never as a path. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxPlaceDeclaredTest::test_place_in_a_part_with_declared_rules_names_their_layer_of_the_closest_folder` |
| REQ-CLI-024 | init --sexp summarises the target and the missing folders per part (details with --details); align and place print text with --human; place calls an existing empty folder present; advice prints one line for the parts without advice; outside git the build says plainly once that .gitignore rules are not applied. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxOutputTest::test_outputs_for_people_are_text_and_init_sexp_is_summarised` |
| REQ-CLI-025 | the package ships no word-vector table (no vectors folder, no .i8 or .npy file under scripts/ or src/), and neither `wingbox init` nor the builder knows --synonyms: both refuse it the way they refuse any unknown option, and init writes nothing. | `tests/test_cli.py::WingboxInitTest::test_no_word_vector_table_and_no_synonyms_option` |
| REQ-CLI-037 | `wingbox add`, `wingbox add csharp`, `wingbox add java go ruby`, `wingbox add x --from FILE`, an unknown language and an unknown option all exit 0 and print one line: the parsers of every language are installed with Wingbox since this version, there is nothing to add; with --sexp the line is one fact, `(nothing-to-add ...)`; nothing goes to stderr; `wingbox add --help` prints the usage; `wingbox --help` keeps one line that names the command as kept for compatibility. | `tests/test_add_command.py::AddCommandTest::test_every_form_of_the_command_says_there_is_nothing_to_add_and_exits_0` |
| REQ-CLI-038 | `wingbox add --list` prints one fact per language before the line that there is nothing to add: `parser-installed` from the environment when every pinned package of its parser is there (built-in for a language parsed without one), else `parser-missing` with what is wrong (absent, or another version) and the command that installs it: the pip extra of the language, or a reinstall of Wingbox for a language without an extra. | REQ-CLI-039 | `wingbox impact a b 2` prints `(graph :built <time> :files <n> :stale 0)` for a graph that is up to date, then one form per file in the order given; an unknown file gives its error form in place and exit status 1, and the other answers are printed. `--json` carries the same under "graph" and "impact"; `--human` prints one text block per file. | `tests/test_output_formats_cli.py::WingboxImpactCommandTest::test_several_files_in_one_call_with_the_state_of_the_graph` |
| REQ-CLI-040 | with WINGBOX_AUTO_REFRESH=0 and one file changed since the build, the graph fact of `wingbox impact a b` says `:stale 1`; known_stale gives 0 for a fresh graph, the number of changed files for a stale one and nothing for a graph that was not built from this folder. | `tests/test_output_formats_cli.py::WingboxImpactCommandTest::test_a_stale_graph_says_how_many_files_it_misses` |

The calls without a form option that printed text for people (`impact`, `usages`, `advice`, `align`, `compare`, `place`) print that text with --human. | REQ-CLI-044 | with --json a call that fails as a whole prints one JSON object on standard output whose first key is `error`, an object with `code` and `message`: no graph in the folder (`no-graph`, exit 1), a depth out of range (`invalid-argument`, exit 2), a name nothing matches for `usages` and `quick` (`no-match`), a git reference that does not exist for `diff_functions` (`git-diff-failed`, with the whole message of git in `message`), and an architecture command without a graph. | REQ-CLI-046 | a fact whose quoted text holds line breaks (the message of a failed git command) becomes one JSON object, not one object per line, and `with_error` takes the first error fact of an answer as its error, or the fallback when the answer is empty. | `tests/test_json_errors.py::JsonErrorsTest::test_a_fact_that_runs_over_several_lines_is_one_object` |

| REQ-CLI-049 | the rule `/.wingbox/` (`.wingbox/` in Git's global excludes file) is appended only to a file that has no rule about `.wingbox` yet: a file with `.wingbox/*` followed by exceptions, with `!/.wingbox/`, `**/.wingbox/` or `.wingbox` is left byte for byte as it is, so `wingbox init` does not override what a team wrote to commit `.wingbox/config.yaml`; a comment that mentions the folder and a rule about another name that only contains it are no such rule. | `tests/test_review_wp8_writes_install.py::ProjectIgnoreTest::test_a_gitignore_that_names_wingbox_is_left_as_it_is` |

## CMP

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-CMP-001 | `wingbox compare` lists each part with its language, style and layers, and the differences within a language: a layer some parts lack, a folder name that means another layer elsewhere, and a style that differs from the majority, which also gets an advice item. | `tests/test_compare.py::CompareTest::test_compare_shows_styles_layers_and_differences_per_language` |
| REQ-CMP-002 | `wingbox compare` never answers with nothing: when no part has a style and a language (a repository of one part without a manifest), it prints one `compare-none` fact with the number of parts of the repository and the reason. --human says the same in words, --json holds the fact and --sexp prints it too. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::CompareTest::test_compare_says_when_there_is_nothing_to_compare` |
| REQ-CMP-003 | when a part is shown, `wingbox compare` prints no `compare-none`: the answer for a repository of one part with a style and a manifest is its `compare-part` line and nothing else, as before. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::CompareTest::test_compare_of_a_part_it_shows_prints_no_compare_none` |

## CPP

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-CPP-001 | a C++ header gives its includes as written, quoted ones plain and angle ones in their brackets, also inside a conditional block, each with its line; classes with their methods and base classes, structs, enums, type aliases, function declarations inside namespaces and macros that have a value; an include guard and `#pragma once` give nothing, and a class with a pure virtual method is not reported as an interface with providers: the dependencies of C and C++ files are their includes. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppParserTest::test_a_header_gives_its_includes_types_functions_and_macros` |
| REQ-CPP-002 | a C++ source file gives the functions it defines, inside a namespace or not, with their lines; a method defined outside its class (`void Painter::paint()`) is the entity `Painter.paint`, the form of every method that has a line of its own. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppParserTest::test_a_source_file_gives_its_function_definitions` |
| REQ-CPP-003 | a `.c` file is a file of the language `c`: its includes, its functions, its `typedef struct` and its structs are read; names that are keywords of C++ only (`new`, `class`) do not stop the parse. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppParserTest::test_a_c_file_is_read_with_the_same_parser` |
| REQ-CPP-004 | the files `.c .cc .cpp .cxx .h .hh .hpp .hxx .inl .ipp` belong to the language; a module id is the path with its extension, so `painter.h` and `painter.cpp` are two modules; the language server is clangd. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppParserTest::test_the_language_owns_the_files_of_c_and_cpp` |
| REQ-CPP-005 | `#include "shape.h"` is the file of that name in the folder of the including file, also when an include folder of the compilation database holds a file of the same name; `#include <shape.h>` is never looked for beside the file. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppIncludeTest::test_a_quoted_include_is_looked_for_beside_the_file_first` |
| REQ-CPP-006 | with a `compile_commands.json` at the root or in `build/`, an include is looked for in the include folders of the entry of the file, in their order: `-I dir`, `-Idir`, `-isystem dir`, `-iquote dir`, `/Idir`, in `arguments` or in `command`, relative to `directory`; a header has no entry and takes the folders of the entries of the nearest folder; a name no folder holds (`<vector>`) is no dependency. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppIncludeTest::test_the_compilation_database_gives_the_include_folders` |
| REQ-CPP-007 | without a compilation database an include is looked for in the folders that the CMake files of the repository name literally (`target_include_directories`, `include_directories`, with `${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}`, `${CMAKE_CURRENT_LIST_DIR}`, `${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}`, `${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}` or a relative path), then, for a quoted name, as the one file of the repository whose path ends with the written path; a path that two files end with is no dependency, nor is a name in angle brackets or the name of a header of the C or C++ standard library (`<clock.h>`, `"math.h"`) that only the tree would find. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppIncludeTest::test_without_a_database_the_cmake_files_and_the_tree_decide` |
| REQ-CPP-008 | `wingbox impact <header> 2 --json` in a repository of C++ and Python lists the files that include the header at depth 1 and the files that include those at depth 2, each `via` `imports`; the header and the source file of the same stem are two rows; the build counts the files as `cpp`. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppIncludeTest::test_impact_of_a_header_names_the_files_that_include_it` |
| REQ-CPP-009 | `wingbox usages <header>::<name>` lists, with their lines, the uses of a function, a type and a macro of a header in the header itself and in the files that include it directly or through another header: a call is `call`, a type is `type`; a file that does not include the header and has a parameter or a local variable of the same name is not listed. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppUsagesTest::test_usages_of_a_name_follow_the_includes` |
| REQ-CPP-010 | a member written with its class or enum (`Painter::create()`, `Mode::Lit`) is a use of `Painter.create` and of `Mode.Lit`; a method called on an object (`painter->paint()`) is listed as an unresolved call of `Painter.paint`, because the type of the object is not read. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppUsagesTest::test_usages_of_a_member_name_its_class_or_stay_unresolved` |
| REQ-CPP-011 | when two files that a file reaches through its includes both declare a function (the same prototype in two headers, or the prototype in a header and the definition in a source file that is included), a call in that file is a use of that function, and `usages` of the name, or of the name in any of the files that declare it, lists it once with the other uses of the function. A name of another kind that two reachable headers both define (a type) stays attributed to neither. Until 0.19.0 the function was attributed to neither as well, and its calls were missing from every answer. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppUsagesTest::test_a_function_two_reachable_headers_declare_is_one_function` |
| REQ-CPP-012 | an include found through the include folders of the CMake files, or as the one file whose path ends with the name, is an edge with `basis` `inferred`; an include found beside the including file or in an include folder of `compile_commands.json` has no `basis`. | `tests/test_generation_links.py::GenerationLinksTest::test_an_include_found_without_the_compiler_folders_is_inferred` |
| REQ-CPP-013 | a class that derives from a class of another file depends on that file through its include only: a file that names a base class it does not include (a class of the same name declared elsewhere) gets no edge, and the graph of C and C++ files has no edge kind but the includes. | `tests/test_cpp_language.py::CppIncludeTest::test_a_base_class_adds_no_dependency_beyond_the_include` |
| REQ-CPP-014 | `impact` of a C or C++ source file also lists the files that include its header: the header of the same stem in its folder, or one of the same stem that it includes from an include folder. Those rows are at depth 1 with `basis` `paired-header` (`:basis paired-header` in the compact and the flat form), the rows below them are ordinary ones, the header is named in a `(impact-paired-header <file> <header>)` note and stands in `read_first` after the file; the source file itself is not a row. | `tests/test_impact_implementation_files.py::ImplementationFileTest::test_impact_of_a_source_file_lists_the_users_of_its_header` |
| REQ-CPP-015 | `impact` of a C or C++ source file that nothing includes and that has no header of its name has no rows and a `(impact-no-includers <file>)` note; the text for people says that the include graph does not show who calls what the file defines and names `wingbox usages`, where it used to say "No other module depends on it". | `tests/test_impact_implementation_files.py::ImplementationFileTest::test_a_source_file_without_a_header_says_what_the_list_cannot_show` |
| REQ-CPP-016 | a row of `impact` whose file includes a C or C++ header carries the names of that header the file uses, as rows of other languages carry the names of an import: `(:use <name> ...)` in the compact form (at most three names, `:of N` when there are more), the same in `reasons` of JSON and in the text. A file that includes the header and uses no name the graph resolved has no name list. For a source file answered through its header the names are those of the header. | `tests/test_impact_implementation_files.py::ImplementationFileTest::test_a_row_of_an_include_names_what_the_file_uses` |
| REQ-CPP-017 | a function prototype that starts with a macro (`DEPOT_API int crate_start(...);`, an export or visibility macro, also inside `extern "C"`) is a declaration of its header, as a prototype without one is: the calls in the files that include the header are uses of it, and a question that names the header or the source file answers for the function as a whole. Before, such a function had no declaration in the graph and `usages` of it answered "0 uses". | `tests/test_cpp_function_usages.py::FunctionUsagesTest::test_a_prototype_behind_an_export_macro_is_a_declaration` |
| REQ-CPP-018 | `usages <name>` of a C or C++ function that a header declares and a source file that includes this header defines answers for the function, not with two candidates: the uses of the declaration and of the definition together, each place once, `:at` the definition. A declaration and a definition of one name in one file are one function. Functions of one name in files that share no declaring header stay candidates, and listing them does not fail when two of them are in one file. | `tests/test_cpp_function_usages.py::FunctionUsagesTest::test_a_bare_name_answers_for_the_declaration_and_its_definition` |
| REQ-CPP-019 | where the grammar cannot read a stretch of a C or C++ file (two preprocessor branches that open the same block), the functions defined below are still found, by their heading at the margin, and so are their uses; a function that names itself in its own body (it hands itself on as a callback) is a use of that function; a call that follows a label and a preprocessor line, which the grammar reads as a declaration, is a call. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_functions_of_a_file_the_grammar_loses_track_of` |
| REQ-CPP-020 | a function declared or defined inside a macro call (`DEPOT_EXPORT(void heap_put(struct heap* heap, int value)) { ... }`) is a function of its real name, and a prototype that starts with a macro and returns a pointer (`DEPOT_API const char* depot_name(int id);`) is a declaration: their calls are uses. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_functions_declared_through_a_macro` |
| REQ-CPP-021 | a class written with a macro between the keyword and its name (`class CRATE_API Crate { ... };`) is a class with its methods, also those declared after a class with a macro nested in it. A call inside the class or inside `Crate::method` without an object, and a call through `this`, is a use of `Crate.method`; so is a call inside a body written after a macro call whose first argument names the class (`TEST_F(CrateTest, Fills) { Fill(3); }`). A class defined inside a function is a class as well. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_methods_of_a_class_with_an_export_macro` |
| REQ-CPP-022 | `usages <name>` of a name that C or C++ classes declare as a method no longer answers no-match: with one such class it answers for `Class.name`, with several (or a function of the name besides) it lists them as candidates `<file>::<Class>.<name>`. A call on an object whose type is not derived is listed as an unresolved call of the method only from files of C and C++: a call of the same name in a Python file is not. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_a_bare_method_name` |
| REQ-CPP-023 | read as written in real C++: a class with a macro before its name and a base class after it; a method with an attribute macro between its parameters and its body, or after the parameters of its declaration; a prototype whose macro makes the grammar join the return type and the name; a function defined as a friend inside a class (a function of the file, called by its plain name); a method of an outer class named without a call inside a class nested in it; a method called through brackets, `(object.method)()`. A question that names one file of a C or C++ function answers for the function: its prototypes and its definition. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_more_shapes_of_real_code` |
| REQ-CPP-024 | in a file where the grammar meets an error, a class keeps the methods that stand between its braces in the text, also where the grammar ends the class early or takes it for the return type of what follows (a macro before a destructor or before `auto`): the methods are methods of the class, calls among them without an object are uses of them, and a class after it is still read. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_methods_of_a_class_the_grammar_cuts_short` |
| REQ-CPP-025 | a namespace that follows a macro on a line of its own (`NS_BEGIN`, then `namespace tools {`) is read as a namespace: its functions are functions and the names declared in it are no locals of a function. In a file where the grammar met an error, the classes and the headings of their methods are also read from the text. A method declared by a googlemock macro (`MOCK_METHOD(void, on_tick, ());`) is a method of its name. A static method handed on by name as an argument inside a method of its class (`CountIf(list, Passed)`) is a use of it. | `tests/test_cpp_usages_complete.py::CompleteUsagesTest::test_namespaces_mocks_and_methods_handed_on_by_name` |
| REQ-CPP-026 | A compile_commands.json nested deeper than the JSON reader goes, or not UTF-8, gives a warning, not an aborted build. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::ManifestReadingTest::test_a_compilation_database_nested_too_deep_is_reported_and_the_build_goes_on` |
| REQ-CPP-027 | A C++ header with 3,000 #elif branches, at file level and in a class, is parsed: the functions of every branch and the include are those of the file. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::DeepTreeTest::test_a_cpp_conditional_of_three_thousand_branches_is_read` |
| REQ-CPP-028 | With a compilation database for part of the tree, a source file without an entry and without a translation unit at or above its folder finds its includes in the folders the CMake files name, then in every folder of the database: both edges are marked `inferred`; a file with an entry keeps its stated edges. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::CppIncludeFoldersTest::test_a_file_the_database_does_not_cover_takes_the_cmake_folders` |
| REQ-CPP-029 | An include whose name differs from the file only in the case of its letters is an edge marked `inferred` when exactly one file matches; with two such files there is no edge. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::CppIncludeFoldersTest::test_an_include_written_in_another_case_is_found_when_one_file_matches` |
| REQ-CPP-030 | A method named `operator()` is written to the graph quoted, as a function and in the methods of its class, so that it reads back whole: `find_function` names `operator()`, not `operator(`. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::CppOperatorNameTest::test_the_call_operator_reads_back_whole` |
| REQ-CPP-031 | A C++ file of 100 KB is parsed within the bound when it holds one long declaration with `= 0`, a long MOCK_METHOD name or tail, or block comments that never close; and the scans of a text of 100 KB stay within it: the template arguments of a name nested thousands deep, the braces of a text of comments that never close, class headings that never reach a brace. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::LinearScansTest::test_cpp_text_scans_are_linear` |
| REQ-CPP-032 | The C++ text scans keep their answers: a name without its template arguments, a pure virtual method, the method of a googlemock macro, braces outside comments and strings, the classes of a text. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::LinearScansTest::test_the_rewritten_cpp_scans_answer_as_before` |
| REQ-CPP-033 | A C++ file is read up to a block comment that nothing closes: the include and the declarations before it are those of the file. A `/*` in a string, a raw string, a character literal or a line comment opens no comment, and the declarations after it are read. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::LinearScansTest::test_a_block_comment_that_never_closes_ends_the_text_that_is_parsed` |

| REQ-CS-159 | while the use file of a build has no positions, `usages` of a function, a class, a method (resolved and unresolved calls), a type, an enum member and a macro, in Python and in C++, answers with the rows it answers after the whole use file is filled; the question leaves the use file without positions (`:positions no`), so nothing of the repository was walked for it but the files that hold the name. | `tests/test_usages_first_query.py::FirstUsagesTest::test_the_first_answer_is_the_answer_of_the_filled_use_file` |
| REQ-CS-160 | the lines read for one name come from the files whose text holds the name and from no other: for `make` of the Python package, the three files that write it; a name no file holds gives no line; a file that changed since the build makes the reader refuse, as the whole fill does. | `tests/test_usages_first_query.py::FirstUsagesTest::test_only_the_files_that_hold_the_name_are_read` |
| REQ-CS-161 | the first question that needs positions starts one process that fills the whole use file and does not wait for it: the answer comes first, the use file says `:positions yes` soon after and `.wingbox/positions.lock` is gone; a second start while the lock is fresh starts nothing, and `WINGBOX_POSITIONS_FILL=0` starts nothing at all. | `tests/test_usages_first_query.py::FirstUsagesTest::test_the_whole_use_file_is_filled_in_a_process_of_its_own` |
| REQ-CS-162 | `wingbox cycles` needs no layer style: it prints one `(cycle N :files K <file> ...)` fact per group of files that depend on each other through imports (two files that import each other, or a longer ring), the largest group first, its files in path order; a file outside every group is in none; the last fact `(cycles-summary :cycles N :files-in-cycles K ...)` counts them, and a repository without such a group prints `:cycles 0`. | `tests/test_cycles_command.py::CyclesCommandTest::test_cycles_names_the_files_that_depend_on_each_other` |
| REQ-CS-163 | `wingbox cycles` also prints `(hub <file> :dependents N :dependencies M)` for a file that at least ten files depend on and that depends on at least ten; `(folder-cycle <a> <b> :forward N :back M)` for two top-level folders with dependencies in both directions, with the count of file pairs each way; and `(folder-order (<folder> ...) ...)`, the top-level folders from those that depend on others to those the others depend on, folders that depend on each other in one group. `wingbox cycles <folder>` answers for the files below that folder, with its subfolders as the folders. `--json` gives the same facts. | `tests/test_cycles_command.py::CyclesCommandTest::test_cycles_names_hubs_and_folders` |
| REQ-CS-164 | a build reads the build targets and the tests the CMake files state literally: `add_executable` and `add_library` with their source files, `target_sources`, and `add_test` in both forms, with the target a test runs (its command, or a `$<TARGET_FILE:<target>>` among its words) and the files of the repository its command names. Source paths are taken from the folder of the list file that includes the CMake file, from `${CMAKE_CURRENT_LIST_DIR}`, `${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}`, `${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}` and `${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}`. The graph holds one `(target <name> :kind executable\|library ...)` and one `(ctest <name> ...)` fact for each; an alias, an imported target and a name written with a variable are left out. | `tests/test_build_targets.py::BuildTargetsTest::test_the_cmake_files_give_targets_and_tests` |
| REQ-CS-165 | `wingbox impact <file> --only tests` in a repository with such facts also names the build targets that hold the file or one of its dependents among their sources, `(affected-targets <file> <target> ...)`, and the tests that run one of those targets or a file of the list, `(affected-tests <file> <test> ...)` (`targets` and `tests` in JSON, two lines in the text); a file no target holds names none, and without `--only tests` the answer is as before. With `--diff --only tests` each `(changed ...)` fact also carries `:targets (...) :tests (...)`, those of the file and of the files that use the changed function. | `tests/test_build_targets.py::BuildTargetsTest::test_only_tests_names_the_targets_and_tests_a_change_reaches` |
| REQ-CS-166 | `wingbox impact <file> [depth] --summary` answers in one short form instead of the complete list: `(impact-summary <file> :direct N :total M :depth D` with `(depths N ...)`, the number of dependents at each depth; `(kinds :generated N :tests N :entry-points N :other N)`, every dependent in one kind (made from the file or started by it; a test file; a file nothing else depends on; any other); `(folders (<folder> N) ... [:more N])`, the eight folders with the most dependents and the number of dependents elsewhere; `(first <file> ... [:more N])`, at most twenty files, nearest depth first and within a depth the generated files, the entry points, the tests, then the others; and `(full "<command>")`, the command that prints the complete list. | REQ-CS-167 | `--summary` with `--json` gives the same content per file (`summary` true, `direct`, `total`, `depth`, `depths`, `kinds`, `folders`, `more_in_other_folders`, `first`, `more`, `full`), with `--human` a text of a few lines, and with `--only tests` it counts and names the test files alone and, where the graph has build targets and tests, adds how many of them the change reaches. A file that is not in the graph answers as without the option; `--summary` with `--sexp` is an error. | `tests/test_impact_summary.py::ImpactSummaryTest::test_the_summary_in_the_other_forms` |
| REQ-CS-168 | a git ref that starts with `-` is refused with `(error invalid-argument ...)` and a non-zero exit status before Git is started, by `wingbox diff_functions <ref>`, by `wingbox query '(diff-functions "<ref>")'` and by `wingbox impact --diff <ref>` (also a one-dash word such as `-R`); the MCP tools `list_changes` and `query_graph` answer the same text as a tool error. `--output=<file>` as the ref leaves that file as it was: Git does not create or empty it. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::RefIsNeverAnOptionTest::test_a_ref_that_starts_with_a_dash_is_refused_before_git_starts` |
| REQ-CS-169 | when Git cannot give the diff (a ref that does not exist), `wingbox diff_functions <ref>` prints one line, `(error git-diff-failed "<the message of Git on one line>")`, and exits with status 1, as `impact --diff <ref>` does; with `--json` the error object carries the code and the message. A ref that names a file as well as a branch is read as the ref. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::RefIsNeverAnOptionTest::test_a_failed_git_diff_is_one_error_line_and_a_failed_command` |
| REQ-CS-170 | `impact --diff` and `diff_functions` name the changed function whatever the Git configuration says about the look of a diff, in the repository's own configuration and in the user's global one: `diff.noprefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix`, `diff.srcPrefix`, `color.diff = always`, `color.ui = always`, `diff.external`, `diff.renames = false`, `diff.relative`, `diff.interHunkContext`; the external diff program is not started. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_the_git_configuration_does_not_change_what_is_read` |
| REQ-CS-171 | a changed file whose path holds a space is named as it is (Git ends its `+++` line with a tab, which is no part of the name) and gets its `impact --diff` answer; a change under a hunk header whose context text holds `+1,` (`@@ -6 +6 @@ export function move(dx = +1, dy = 0) {`) is read from the part between the two `@@` alone and names the function. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_a_path_with_a_space_and_a_header_that_holds_a_plus_sign` |
| REQ-CS-172 | the text of a patch is read exactly: the lines of a hunk are counted by its header and never read as headers (a removed line `-- a/x` and an added line `++ b/x` look like file headers; a line may hold a form feed or a carriage return); a quoted name is unquoted (`\t`, `\"`, `\\`, an octal escape); the tab after a name with a space is dropped; a file without a text patch (a mode change, a binary file, a rename without a change, an empty new file) is named from its `diff --git` or `rename` lines; a removed file is `deleted`, a renamed one carries the path it had, and a file removed and created again in one diff (a type change) is one changed file. Removed lines count for the line above them; a change before line 1 gives no range. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_a_patch_is_read_by_its_structure` |
| REQ-CS-173 | with the graph built in a folder below the top level of the Git repository, `impact --diff` and `diff_functions` answer for the changed files of that folder, by their paths relative to the graph root; a change of a file outside that folder is no change of the graph (`impact-diff-empty`, `diff-functions-empty`) and is never mapped to a file of the graph whose path ends the same way (a changed `util.py` at the top level is not `a/util.py` of the graph). | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_a_graph_below_the_top_of_the_repository_and_an_exact_path` |
| REQ-CS-174 | a changed line is mapped to the definition that starts above it, and the last definition of a file runs to the end of the file: a change at line 251 of a file with one class names that class, not `diff-functions-no-match`. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_the_last_definition_of_a_file_runs_to_its_end` |
| REQ-CS-175 | a file from which lines were only removed is a changed file: `impact --diff` gives its impact answer and `diff_functions` names it, never the empty form. | REQ-CS-176 | a diff that removes a file (`git rm`) or renames one (`git mv`) never answers the empty form. `impact --diff` names the path that is gone with `(changed-file <path> :status deleted :dependents unknown)` or `(changed-file <old path> :status renamed :to <new path> :dependents unknown)` after the impact answers (`changed_files` in JSON, a block in the text for people, the same fact under `--summary`), answers for the new path of a rename, and exits with status 0; a diff that only removes files prints these facts alone. `diff_functions` names the same paths with `(diff-file <path> :hunks N :status deleted)` and `(diff-file <old path> :hunks N :status renamed :to <new path>)`. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::RemovedAndRenamedTest::test_a_removed_file_and_a_renamed_file_are_named` |
| REQ-CS-177 | under `impact --diff --only tests` a changed function that no test file uses directly and that other files do use carries `:other-users N`, the number of those files, after `:used-by ()` (`other_users` in JSON, the same key on `changed-summary`, and the text for people says it), so that the empty list does not read as "nothing uses it". A function a test file uses, a function nothing uses and every answer without `--only tests` are printed as before. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::ChangedUnderOnlyTestsTest::test_a_function_only_other_files_use_says_so` |
| REQ-CS-178 | for a diff with awkward names and changes (a space, letters outside ASCII, a rename with and without a change, a removed file, a binary file, a new file, a removed line that looks like a file header and, where the file system takes them, a tab, a double quote and a backslash in a name), the files and the status read from the patch are exactly those `git diff --raw -z` lists, which Git writes without quoting. | `tests/test_review_wp1_git_diff.py::DiffIsReadExactlyTest::test_the_files_read_from_a_patch_are_those_git_names` |
| REQ-CS-179 | `rdeps <id> <depth>` lists every module that depends on the module within the depth, each on one line without `:cycle` at its shortest depth: a module that is reached through a longer path first (at the last depth, where it is not followed further) is listed and followed at its shorter depth, so the files are those of `impact` for the same depth. | `tests/test_review_wp2_query.py::DependencyTreeTest::test_rdeps_lists_every_dependent_within_the_depth` |
| REQ-CS-180 | `deps <id> <depth>` lists every module the module depends on within the depth, each at its shortest depth, also one that a longer path reaches first; the `dep` lines of `analyze` (depth 2) and the `calls-module` lines of `find_calls` are the same walk. | `tests/test_review_wp2_query.py::DependencyTreeTest::test_deps_lists_every_dependency_within_the_depth` |
