| REQ-LANG-015 | two words are within one edit exactly when their edit distance is at most 1: for 40,000 generated pairs (equal, one or two edits apart, of different lengths, empty) and for every role suffix against every tail of every class name of this repository. | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::PurposeTest::test_one_edit_is_the_edit_distance_of_one` |
| REQ-LANG-016 | the purpose of every Python file of this repository, of the same files under the other languages' rules, and of files that hold class names one edit away from a role suffix, is the one worked out with every name compared with every suffix by the full edit distance and nothing kept. | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::PurposeTest::test_every_purpose_is_the_one_the_full_comparison_gives` |
| REQ-LANG-017 | for 60 generated Python and TypeScript projects (facades of several levels, a name defined twice behind a facade, re-export cycles, private names, chains longer than the depth limit), what every name means to an importer of every module, the definition every use site resolves to, and the resolved uses of every module are the same from the search that follows only the glob imports reaching a holder of the name as from the search that follows every glob import; in any order of questions. | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::GlobSearchTest::test_the_search_behind_glob_imports_answers_as_the_exhaustive_one` |
| REQ-LANG-018 | the search for the class that defines a method visits each class once, also when classes name each other as bases: twelve classes that each name the other eleven answer at once, where the search used to walk every chain of sixteen bases; a method of a base class is still found, the nearest base in the order written first. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::MemberUseTests::test_classes_that_name_each_other_as_bases_end_the_search` |

| REQ-LEARN-001 | `wingbox learn` prints, per part, the closest style with score and runner-up, its layers with their meaning, the folders it places, each folder it does not place with real files, imports by layer and a name guess, then the questions, the rules and the exact answer format and the apply command. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_the_task_names_style_layers_unplaced_folders_files_imports_and_the_format` |
| REQ-LEARN-002 | a valid answer is written into .wingbox/config.yaml with `learned by agent: <because>`, logged in decisions.sexp, and the result names what changed and the violation counts after the rebuild. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_a_valid_answer_is_written_as_learned_and_reported` |
| REQ-LEARN-003 | an answer with an unknown layer, a folder the part does not have, a folder twice, an unknown style or a malformed line is rejected as a whole, with one error per problem, and nothing is written. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_an_invalid_answer_is_rejected_whole_with_every_error` |
| REQ-LEARN-004 | a folder the user already maps in .wingbox/config.yaml keeps its layer; the answer for it is reported as kept. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_an_answer_never_overwrites_an_entry_of_the_user` |
| REQ-LEARN-005 | an accepted answer is appended to .wingbox/learned.sexp with date, mode, agent, style, score and each because; .wingbox/.gitignore keeps the generated files out (index, cache) and leaves config.yaml, learned.sexp, decisions.sexp and baseline.sexp visible to git; the next `learn` lists the folder as known instead of asking again, unless --again; a rejected answer saves nothing. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_what_learn_saves_in_dot_wingbox_and_reuses` |
| REQ-LEARN-006 | run by a person (--human or a terminal), learn asks the style of each part among its candidates (score and a one-line description; Enter keeps the closest), then one question per open folder with its files and imports (an invalid layer is asked again); it records the answers like an agent's, and at the end offers to open .wingbox/config.yaml in \$VISUAL or \$EDITOR. --yes takes the closest style and the guesses without asking and opens nothing. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_a_person_in_a_terminal_answers_a_questionnaire_and_may_open_the_config` |
| REQ-LEARN-007 | --agent and --human win; then CLAUDECODE=1 or GEMINI_CLI=1 mean an agent; then stdin or stdout without a terminal means an agent and a terminal means a person; the reason is returned to be printed. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnModeTest::test_flags_then_agent_variables_then_terminals_decide` |
| REQ-LEARN-008 | the terminal test uses the real file descriptors: a pseudo-terminal counts as a person, a pipe as an agent. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnModeTest::test_a_pseudo_terminal_is_a_person_and_a_pipe_an_agent` |
| REQ-LEARN-009 | the task also lists nested folders whose files break a rule or rest on a weak label, at the deepest folder holding the files, most breaks first and capped; an answer for such a folder path relabels exactly that folder and its breaks go away. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_nested_folders_that_break_rules_are_asked_and_an_answer_relabels_them` |
| REQ-LEARN-010 | `wingbox learn --apply` rebuilds quietly: no [code-graph] lines of the build or of its parser processes reach stdout or stderr unless --verbose. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_apply_prints_no_build_lines_of_the_parser_workers` |
| REQ-LEARN-011 | `wingbox align --learn` run by a person (--human or a terminal) runs the questionnaire of learn; run by an agent or without a terminal it keeps writing what it derives. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_align_learn_at_a_terminal_runs_the_questionnaire` |
| REQ-LEARN-012 | the learn task says that when the code does not decide, the agent asks the operator in its chat and passes the answer on (:because "operator: ..."), and never guesses. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_the_task_tells_the_agent_to_ask_the_operator` |
| REQ-LEARN-013 | .wingbox/config.yaml starts with a header of instructions (each key, the rule that decides a file's layer, how to move a folder, baseline, run build and boundaries after a change, run align --learn when unsure); every write puts the current header on top once and keeps the parts below it. | `tests/test_learn.py::LearnTest::test_the_config_starts_with_instructions_rewritten_on_every_write` |
| REQ-LEARN-014 | \$VISUAL/\$EDITOR becomes an argument list: POSIX shell words on macOS and Linux; on Windows a quoted program path with spaces and backslashes stays whole, and notepad is used when nothing is set; elsewhere nothing set means no editor. | `tests/test_learn.py::EditorCommandTest::test_the_editor_setting_becomes_an_argument_list_on_every_system` |

| REQ-LEARN-016 | `wingbox learn --apply` with `(style . hexagonal)` on a part whose config lists the layers of another style writes the folders the new style places, not the old `layers:` block: the config passes the check, the build after it succeeds and the exit status is 0. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::ConfigWriteTest::test_a_style_change_through_learn_gives_a_config_the_build_reads` |

| REQ-LEARN-018 | before `wingbox learn --apply` replaces `.wingbox/config.yaml`, the text is checked the way the build checks the file; a text the check refuses (here: a capability rule that names a layer the new style does not have) is not written: the command names the reason, exits 1 and leaves config.yaml and learned.sexp as they were. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::ConfigWriteTest::test_learn_writes_nothing_when_the_config_would_not_pass_the_check` |

| REQ-LEARN-021 | `wingbox learn --apply FILE` with a file that does not exist, or that is not UTF-8, names the file and the reason on stderr and exits 2; nothing is saved. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::ConfigWriteTest::test_an_answer_file_that_cannot_be_read_is_an_error` |

| REQ-MCP-002 | a request carrying `_meta` protocol fields must be served by the stateless revision 2026-07-28: `server/discover` lists the supported versions, results carry `resultType`, an unsupported version gets -32022 with the supported list, a request without client capabilities gets -32602. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_requests_with_per_request_metadata_follow_the_stateless_revision` |
| REQ-MCP-003 | `tools/list` must name the tool that builds the graph (`build_graph`) and the read commands, each with a one-line description and a JSON object input schema. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_tools_list_describes_every_tool_for_an_agent` |
| REQ-MCP-004 | the `build_graph` tool must build the code graph of --root, and `analyze_impact` must then return the answer of `wingbox impact` (its default S-expression form, REQ-MCP-033) only, as one text item. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_build_then_impact_return_text_and_s_expressions` |
| REQ-MCP-005 | a query tool called before any graph exists must return a tool error that tells the agent to call `build_graph` first. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_a_query_without_a_graph_asks_for_build` |
| REQ-MCP-006 | malformed JSON must get -32700, a message that is not a request object -32600, and the server must go on answering the next request. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_malformed_input_gets_json_rpc_errors_and_the_server_keeps_running` |
| REQ-MCP-007 | an unknown method must get -32601, an unknown tool -32602, and arguments that do not match the input schema a tool result with isError true. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_unknown_methods_tools_and_bad_arguments` |
| REQ-MCP-008 | every line `wingbox mcp` writes to stdout must be one JSON-RPC 2.0 message, also while it builds; logs go to stderr. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_stdout_carries_only_json_rpc_messages` |
| REQ-MCP-009 | a tool answer without its own list limit must stop at 200 lines and say how many were left out, unless the call sets all. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpAnswerLimitTest::test_answers_without_their_own_limit_stop_at_the_answer_limit` |
| REQ-MCP-010 | tool arguments must be checked against the tool's input schema: required names, types, integer bounds and unknown names. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpAnswerLimitTest::test_arguments_are_checked_against_the_input_schema` |
| REQ-MCP-011 | every tool in `tools/list` must carry MCP annotations with a short `title`, `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint` and `openWorldHint` false; `build` is the only tool that is not read-only (it writes the graph), and it is neither destructive nor open-world and is idempotent. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_tools_carry_annotations` |
| REQ-MCP-012 | every tool name must be shorter than 64 characters and use only letters, digits, underscores and hyphens. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_tool_names_fit_the_directory_limit` |
| REQ-MCP-013 | server.json, the entry of the MCP Registry, names the PyPI package at the version of pyproject.toml, starts the server with `wingbox mcp` over stdio, and keeps the registry's limits (name pattern, description of at most 100 characters). | `tests/test_mcp_registry.py::McpRegistryTest::test_server_json_matches_the_package` |
| REQ-MCP-014 | the README, which PyPI shows as the package description, carries `mcp-name: <server name>` followed by a boundary, so the MCP Registry can verify who owns the package. | `tests/test_mcp_registry.py::McpRegistryTest::test_readme_carries_the_ownership_line` |
| REQ-MCP-015 | `wingbox mcp --root DIR --build-missing` (what the Claude Desktop extension starts) builds the graph of DIR in the background when it has none; a tool call waits for that build up to WINGBOX_FIRST_BUILD_WAIT seconds (default 45) and then answers from the new graph. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_with_build_missing_the_first_query_builds_the_graph` |
| REQ-MCP-016 | when the first build takes longer than WINGBOX_FIRST_BUILD_WAIT seconds (a large repository), a tool call returns at once with a tool error that says the graph of DIR is being built for the first time and to call again shortly; the build goes on in the background. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_a_first_build_longer_than_the_wait_is_reported_not_hung` |
| REQ-MCP-017 | without --build-missing the server never builds on its own (the plugin starts it in whatever folder the session runs in); an unknown argument exits 2 with the usage. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_without_build_missing_nothing_is_built` |
| REQ-MCP-018 | two `wingbox mcp --build-missing` started at the same moment on a repository without a graph (Claude Desktop starts its server twice) run one build: the other server says `[wingbox] a build is already running: waiting for it`, no line contains ERROR, and both answer impact from the graph. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_two_servers_started_together_build_once_without_an_error` |
| REQ-MCP-019 | started with --build-missing in a folder that is in no source repository (no .git or project manifest with source files, as when an app starts the server in the home folder), the server builds nothing and every tool answers that there is no repository here. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_a_folder_that_is_no_repository_is_never_built` |
| REQ-MCP-020 | after `build_graph`, the `describe_module` tool (the `analyze` command) prints the validation mark of a data-flow fact with its evidence for a module that imports a pydantic model, and no data-flow line for a module that imports a file whose comment only names a validator; no answer holds the retired mark `:validated`. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_module_states_validation_only_with_the_evidence_of_the_stored_fact` |
| REQ-MCP-021 | the description of every tool starts with a verb that says what the tool does ("Lists ...", "Traces ..."), says when to use the tool ("Use it ..."), names the neighbour to use for another question as "use `name`" (build_graph has no neighbour), and names every parameter of the tool except `all` in backticks, so that it says more about them than the schema. A tool that takes `all` says that a list stops at 200 items; a tool that looks a name up says what an unknown name returns. A description is at most 900 characters and sends a caller to existing tools only. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_every_description_says_what_comes_back_and_when_to_use_a_neighbour` |
| REQ-MCP-022 | a call of a removed tool name gets the unknown-tool error (-32602), as any unknown tool. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_a_removed_tool_is_an_unknown_tool` |
| REQ-MCP-023 | `dependencies` gives what a module uses (direction out, the default) or who uses it (direction in), hop by hop; any other direction is refused by the input schema. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::MergedToolsTest::test_dependencies_follows_a_module_out_or_in` |
| REQ-MCP-024 | `calls` gives who calls a function (direction in, the default), what it calls (out), or, with `to`, a call path from the function to another one. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::MergedToolsTest::test_calls_follows_a_function_in_out_or_to_another` |
| REQ-MCP-025 | `definitions` says where a name is defined, with file and line: kind function or type narrows it; without a kind it finds functions, types and constants, and prints definitions only (no list of the modules that refer to the name). | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::MergedToolsTest::test_definitions_finds_functions_types_and_constants` |
| REQ-MCP-026 | `modules` analyzes the module it is given by ID or file path; given a word that is no module it analyzes the best matching module; a word that matches nothing is a tool error. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::MergedToolsTest::test_module_takes_a_module_or_a_keyword` |
| REQ-MCP-027 | `changes` lists the functions changed in the working files against a git ref (default HEAD). | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::MergedToolsTest::test_changes_lists_the_functions_changed_since_a_ref` |
| REQ-MCP-028 | every parameter of every tool has a description of at least ten characters; a parameter that is a choice lists its values (`enum`) and has a default among them; an integer parameter has a lower and an upper bound. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_every_parameter_is_described_and_every_choice_is_closed` |
| REQ-MCP-029 | `tools/list` offers exactly fourteen tools (within the 3 to 15 a tool directory grades best), in this order: build_graph, analyze_impact, describe_module, list_files, read_source, search_source, find_definitions, find_usages, list_dependencies, trace_calls, list_layers, check_boundaries, list_changes, query_graph. Every name is a verb and a noun in lowercase snake_case; no name is a prefix of another; no name a tool had before is left. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_the_server_offers_fourteen_tools_named_verb_noun` |
| REQ-MCP-030 | the instructions the server gives at `initialize` and `server/discover` name tools of the current list only: no earlier tool name is left in them as a tool to call, and they name analyze_impact, describe_module, find_usages, read_source and build_graph. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_the_instructions_name_only_tools_that_exist` |
| REQ-MCP-031 | the expression `(help)`, which the description of `query_graph` names, returns the list of the functions of the query language and is not an error. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_facts_lists_its_functions` |
| REQ-MCP-032 | `build_graph` builds the code graph and is the only tool that writes, `describe_module` describes a module and `query_graph` evaluates a query; every name a tool had before (`build`, `graph`, `impact`, `modules`, `facts`, ...) is an unknown tool. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_only_the_new_names_answer` |
| REQ-MCP-033 | the MCP `impact` tool takes `target` (one file) or `targets` (a list); it returns the default form of `wingbox impact`, complete: one form per file in the order given after the graph fact (each followed by the advice that concerns the file), the error form in place of a file that is not in the graph; with neither argument the answer is an error. `all` is still accepted and changes nothing. | `tests/test_output_formats_cli.py::McpImpactTest::test_the_impact_tool_takes_a_list_and_returns_the_default_form` |
| REQ-MCP-034 | `list_files` lists the source files of the graph, all or those whose path matches `pattern`; `read_source` returns the numbered lines of a file of the graph, of a range of it, or of one definition. A path outside the repository, a file that is not in the graph and an unknown name are tool errors, and no line of any file is returned for them. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_files_and_source_are_read_through_the_server` |
| REQ-MCP-035 | the number of lines the description of `read_source` names is the limit of the `source` command. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_the_line_limit_of_read_source_is_the_one_of_the_command` |
| REQ-MCP-036 | `search_source` returns the lines of the source files of the graph that hold a text, each with its file and line number, in all files or in those whose path matches `path`. A text no line holds is an empty answer, not an error; a pattern without text is a tool error. A file that is not a source file of the graph is never searched. An answer that is cut names the next match, and `first` goes on from it. The limits its description names are those of the `grep` command. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_text_is_searched_through_the_server` |
| REQ-MCP-037 | `query_graph` answers a malformed expression (a parenthesis that is not closed, a function the language does not have) with a tool error whose text says what is wrong, as its description says; a selection that holds nothing is an empty result and no error. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_a_malformed_query_is_a_tool_error` |
| REQ-MCP-038 | `analyze_impact` with `summary` true answers with the `(impact-summary ...)` form of `wingbox impact --summary` (the counts, the folders, the first files and the command of the complete list) and without the rows of the complete form; without `summary` the answer is the complete form. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_the_summary_of_an_impact_through_the_server` |
| REQ-MCP-039 | `analyze_impact` with `only` set to `tests` answers with the form `wingbox impact --only tests` prints (`:only tests`, the test files alone) and is no tool error; together with `summary` true it answers with the short form of the test files. Version 0.18.0 refused the call: the server checked the arguments as a command line that knows no `--only`. | `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::ToolListTest::test_the_tests_of_an_impact_through_the_server` |
| REQ-MCP-040 | the tools `describe_module`, `list_dependencies` and `check_boundaries` take the path of a file of the graph where they take a module ID, as their descriptions say, and answer for that module; the path of a file that is not in the graph is a tool error, never the answer of another file. | `tests/test_review_wp2_query.py::McpFilePathTest::test_the_tools_that_take_a_module_take_its_file_path` |
| REQ-MCP-041 | started without --build-missing in a folder that is in no source repository (source files, but no .git and no project manifest at or above it), `build_graph` answers what every other tool answers there, a tool error that says there is no repository here, and writes no .wingbox/ folder. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_build_graph_builds_nothing_where_there_is_no_repository` |
| REQ-MCP-042 | --root is where the search for the code graph starts, not a boundary: `wingbox mcp --root <project>/shop` serves the graph of &lt;project>, and its first log line and its `instructions` name &lt;project> (the log line also the --root it was given); started at the project folder itself, both name that folder only. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_the_server_names_the_project_whose_graph_it_serves` |
| REQ-MCP-043 | a line of 200,000 opening brackets, which the JSON reader gives up on with a recursion error, is answered -32700 with a null id like any other text that is not JSON; the server goes on and answers the next request, and exits 0 at the end of its input. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_a_message_nested_too_deeply_is_a_parse_error_and_the_server_goes_on` |
| REQ-MCP-044 | an empty string as an item of `targets` (analyze_impact) or of `exclude_dirs` (build_graph) is refused as an invalid argument that names the parameter, as an empty `target`, `symbol`, `scope` or `ref` is: no file is answered for in its place, and nothing is built; the input schema states the rule (`minLength` 1 for the items). | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_an_empty_item_of_a_list_argument_is_refused` |
| REQ-MCP-045 | through `tools/call`, an answer without its own list limit stops at 200 lines and ends with one line that says how many are hidden and to call again with all=true, and with `all: true` it is complete: for a tool that prints the answer of one command (`list_files` on 230 files) and for `find_definitions` without a kind, which filters the answer of a command first. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_a_tool_answer_of_more_than_200_lines_stops_there_unless_all_is_set` |
| REQ-MCP-046 | the `find_usages` tool must return the text answer of `wingbox usages`: every use of the symbol by file with line:column and kind, as one text item and no S-expression facts. | `tests/test_mcp_server.py::McpProtocolTest::test_usages_returns_the_text_answer` |

| REQ-NEW-005 | a new empty folder inside a repository whose .wingbox/config.yaml has parts of the same language is offered their style first ("same as packages/a"), before the \$HOME favourite: the precedence is declared rules > project config > siblings > home > the library. | `tests/test_new_project.py::NewProjectTest::test_a_new_part_in_a_monorepo_takes_the_style_of_its_siblings_before_home` |

| REQ-NEW-008 | `wingbox init` in an empty folder below the home folder never takes `$HOME/.wingbox/config.yaml` (the favourite styles of a person) for the config of the project: it writes the config, the setup notes and the .gitignore into the new folder and leaves the home folder as it was. | `tests/test_review_wp4_archconfig.py::ProjectRootTest::test_init_never_adopts_the_config_of_the_home_folder` |

| REQ-OUT-017 | in the layers view the map settles the force layout synchronously before drawing (rebuild, layout change and hops change call settle), and choosing a file only redraws: nothing drifts on screen; the graph view keeps its live settling. | `tests/test_overview.py::OverviewTest::test_the_layers_view_is_brought_to_rest_before_it_is_drawn` |
| REQ-OUT-018 | the map opens on the most depended-on file whose 2-hop radius stays under 5 % of the modules; when none qualifies, on the file with the widest radius. | `tests/test_overview.py::OverviewTest::test_the_map_opens_on_a_file_with_a_readable_blast_radius` |

| REQ-OUT-020 | REPORT.md lists, under the key findings, the languages of the repository Wingbox has no parser for with their file counts, and says "not checked" in place of a count of boundary violations when no part of the repository was checked by a rule: zero violations is written only for parts that were checked. | `tests/test_coverage_limits.py::OutsideTheGraphTest::test_the_report_names_what_is_not_covered` |
| REQ-OUT-021 | REPORT.md shows text that comes from the repository as text: the evidence of a capability break, a pattern break or an advice (a line of source) stands in a code span whose fence is longer than any run of backticks in it, with a pipe escaped and a line break as a space, so an image, a link, an HTML tag or comment in the line stays inside one table cell and is not rendered; in the cells for the advice, its style and its rule and in the title, `<` is written `&lt;` and a backslash, `[` and `!` are escaped. | `tests/test_overview.py::OverviewTest::test_text_of_the_repository_is_never_markdown_of_the_report` |
| REQ-OUT-022 | the command REPORT.md prints under "Next steps" is safe to paste into a shell: a file name with spaces or shell characters (`;`, `$`, a quote) is one quoted word of `wingbox impact`, an ordinary path is printed as it is, and a file whose name holds a line break is never put into the command. | `tests/test_overview.py::OverviewTest::test_the_command_of_the_next_steps_is_quoted_for_a_shell` |
| REQ-OUT-023 | the command the map page shows and copies for a selected file, `wingbox impact <file>`, takes the file name through the page's `shellWord`, which gives an ordinary path as it is and any other name as one single-quoted word (a quote inside it closed, escaped and reopened), as Python's `shlex.quote` does; the function is run with node, and that part is skipped, and reported as skipped, where node is not installed. | `tests/test_overview.py::OverviewTest::test_the_command_the_map_copies_is_quoted_for_a_shell` |

## PAS

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-PAS-001 | Pascal parsing preserves architecture-relevant declarations and dependencies. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_parser_extracts_units_uses_types_methods_and_routines` |
| REQ-PAS-002 | Lexical noise cannot create false modules or declarations. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_parser_ignores_keywords_inside_comments_strings_and_bodies` |
| REQ-PAS-003 | Pascal uses clauses resolve by unit identity and explicit relative path. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_graph_resolves_units_case_insensitively_and_honors_in_paths` |
| REQ-PAS-004 | Duplicate Pascal unit names never create guessed dependency edges. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_ambiguous_unit_names_fail_closed` |
| REQ-PAS-005 | Pascal registration owns standard FPC and Lazarus source extensions. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_language_metadata_and_discovery_cover_free_pascal_sources` |
| REQ-PAS-006 | Pascal include fragments cannot collide with compilation units. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_include_and_compilation_unit_with_same_stem_have_distinct_ids` |
| REQ-PAS-007 | Search results return real Pascal source paths for every supported extension. | `tests/test_code_graph_search.py::CodeGraphSearchAliasTest::test_module_paths_resolve_free_pascal_sources_and_includes` |
| REQ-PAS-008 | An explicit but unresolved Pascal path blocks guessed unit-name fallback. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_unresolved_explicit_in_path_does_not_fallback_to_unit_name` |
| REQ-PAS-009 | const/var parameters in procedural types preserve following declarations. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_procedural_type_parameters_do_not_end_the_type_section` |
| REQ-PAS-010 | class-of references remain scalar types and preserve following declarations. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_class_reference_does_not_consume_following_types` |
| REQ-PAS-011 | Generic inheritance records base types without arguments or specialize markers. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_generic_and_specialized_base_types_preserve_outer_names` |
| REQ-PAS-012 | Pascal base types resolve only through case-insensitive used-unit scope. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_inheritance_resolves_case_insensitively_through_used_unit` |
| REQ-PAS-013 | Pascal inheritance never links a globally unique but unimported type. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_inheritance_without_uses_does_not_guess_global_provider` |
| REQ-PAS-014 | Explicit unit paths scope inherited types when names are duplicated. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_explicit_unit_path_disambiguates_inherited_type_provider` |
| REQ-PAS-015 | Competing conditional explicit paths never select an arbitrary provider. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_conditional_explicit_paths_remain_ambiguous` |
| REQ-PAS-016 | Lazarus backup copies are excluded without hiding other languages. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_discovery_ignores_only_pascal_sources_in_lazarus_backup_dirs` |
| REQ-PAS-017 | A local Pascal unit wins over same-named units elsewhere. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_same_directory_unit_wins_over_ambiguous_global_name` |
| REQ-PAS-018 | Include fragments never satisfy a uses-clause unit import. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_same_directory_include_cannot_shadow_compilation_unit` |
| REQ-PAS-019 | Pascal resolver reads surface failures instead of silently publishing partial facts. | `tests/test_pascal_language.py::PascalLanguageTests::test_unreadable_pascal_source_surfaces_resolver_diagnostic` |
| REQ-PAS-020 | A Pascal include directive is read in linear time (100 KB of spaces in one directive within the bound), and as before. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::LinearScansTest::test_a_pascal_include_directive_is_read_in_linear_time` |

| REQ-PHP-012 | `@extends` and `@include` create Blade-to-Blade edges while a reference inside `{{-- ... --}}` creates none. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_blade_extends_and_include_edges_ignore_comments` |

| REQ-PHP-014 | `<livewire:counter>` resolves to App\\Livewire\\Counter and a dotted alias to the module's nested component. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_livewire_tags_resolve_app_and_module_class_conventions` |
| REQ-PHP-015 | `Livewire::component('alias', Class::class)` makes `<livewire:alias>` resolve to that class. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_a_livewire_component_registration_resolves_its_alias` |
| REQ-PHP-016 | a Livewire component class links to its conventional view and a page embedding it depends on both the class and the template. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_a_livewire_component_links_its_conventional_view_and_a_page_both` |

| REQ-PHP-018 | a --exclude-dir vendor style exclusion drops the Composer vendor tree, and a Blade-only project builds. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_vendor_exclusion_keeps_composer_packages_out_and_blade_only_builds` |
| REQ-PHP-019 | anonymous classes, closures and PHP 8 attributes neither crash the parser nor add declarations or method entities. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_anonymous_classes_closures_and_attributes_invent_no_declarations` |
| REQ-PHP-020 | `resources/views/vendor` holds published Laravel views and is discovered, while a Composer vendor directory at the repository root is not. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_a_published_vendor_view_is_source_while_root_vendor_is_excluded` |
| REQ-PHP-021 | an explicit `use` import is authoritative, so an external `Request` import gives no edge to a same-namespace class while an aliased local import still resolves. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_an_explicit_use_import_wins_over_a_same_namespace_class` |
| REQ-PHP-022 | a leading backslash names a global class and survives in the `php-symbol:` import, so `#[\Attribute]` does not depend on a local Livewire attribute class. | `tests/test_php_language.py::PhpLanguageTests::test_an_absolute_class_reference_is_global_and_keeps_its_backslash` |

'/path'`, `dirname(__FILE__)` and `dirname(__DIR__)` name a file by the folder of the requiring file: it is a dependency, with the line; a path of another form gives no edge. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::PhpFunctionsTest::test_a_file_required_by_its_folder_is_a_dependency` |

| REQ-PLUGIN-002 | `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` at the repository root must list the plugin wingbox from ./plugins/wingbox, so that `/plugin marketplace add aileron-tech/wingbox` then `/plugin install wingbox@aileron-tech` installs it. | `tests/test_plugins.py::ClaudePluginTest::test_marketplace` |

| REQ-PLUGIN-004 | after SKILL.md or the version in pyproject.toml changes, `tools/sync_plugin.py --check` must exit 1 and name each stale file, and `tools/sync_plugin.py` must rewrite the skill copy, the version in both manifests and the pinned commit of every uvx command. | `tests/test_plugins.py::SkillCopyTest::test_sync_repairs_drift` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-005 | every uvx command of the plugins (the four hooks and the MCP server of Claude Code, the MCP server of Codex) must name the source as a literal git URL pinned to the same full 40-character commit (the Claude plugin directory refuses a PyPI version, a variable or a short commit as a pin), the commit of a vX.Y.Z tag when the tags are present. | `tests/test_plugins.py::PinTest::test_every_uvx_command_is_pinned_to_a_release_commit` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-010 | the plugin folder must hold no symbolic link, no top-level bin/, no CLAUDE.md, no system file, at most 512 files, no text file over 256 KiB, a README of at least 40 words and the LICENSE. | `tests/test_plugins.py::PluginFolderTest::test_directory_rules` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-011 | the Codex manifest `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` must name the plugin wingbox at the version of pyproject.toml, declare ./skills/ and ./codex.mcp.json (not .mcp.json, which the Claude plugin directory reads as a second, shadowed server), require a local executor, and give the listing fields within the directory limits (display name and subtitle at most 30 characters, at most three starter prompts of 128 characters, square icons that exist); `codex.mcp.json` must start the pinned release with `uvx --quiet --from <pin> wingbox mcp --build-missing` and no `cwd`, so that it runs in the session folder without a `wingbox` on PATH. | `tests/test_plugins.py::CodexPluginTest::test_manifest` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-012 | `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` must list wingbox from the local path ./plugins/wingbox with an install policy, an authentication policy and a category, so that `codex plugin marketplace add aileron-tech/wingbox` offers it. | `tests/test_plugins.py::CodexPluginTest::test_marketplace` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-013 | `tools/build_openai_plugin.py --out DIR` must write the skills-only ZIP wingbox-openai-plugin-&lt;version>.zip (Codex manifest with requires_local_executor and no MCP, skill, icon, the package README of docs/store, LICENSE; no .mcp.json, hooks, .claude-plugin, scripts or bin) and wingbox-openai-plugin-&lt;version>-local-mcp.zip that also holds .mcp.json and declares it. | `tests/test_plugins.py::OpenAIPackageTest::test_zip_packages` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-014 | the short description is one text in every manifest that has one (the Claude Code plugin, the Codex plugin, the Claude Code marketplace entry, the Claude Desktop extension): "A map of your codebase for coding agents: what a change touches, where a symbol is used."; the Codex one-line subtitle (at most 30 characters) is its start, the Codex long description starts with the slogan, and the keywords of both plugin manifests include monorepo, large-codebase, codebase-map, call-graph, refactoring and code-navigation besides the earlier ones. | `tests/test_plugins.py::PurposeTest::test_descriptions_and_keywords` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-015 | the description of SKILL.md must name large repositories and monorepos and the questions an agent hears (what a change will break, who uses a file or function, where a feature is implemented, which files to read first) and the moments (a refactor, a cross-package change), within the 1,024 characters of the Agent Skills format. | `tests/test_plugins.py::PurposeTest::test_skill_description` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-016 | in a copy of the repository without .git or tags (a source archive, a shallow CI clone) `tools/sync_plugin.py --check` must pass, keeping the release the manifest already pins. | `tests/test_plugins.py::NoGitTest::test_check_without_git` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-017 | the plugin follows the newest vX.Y.Z tag that is an ancestor of HEAD, so a branch cut before a release keeps passing when the release tag lands on main; with tags present but none reachable (a shallow clone) the sync stops with a message that says to fetch the tags and the history. | `tests/test_plugins.py::ReleaseTagTest::test_the_release_is_the_newest_tag_that_is_an_ancestor_of_head` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-018 | plugins/wingbox-skill must be a plugin named wingbox at the version of the full plugin, with the same short description, and hold only what the Claude plugin directory takes from an organization it has not verified: no hooks and no mcpServers in the manifest, no hooks.json, no .mcp.json or other MCP configuration, no evals, no file that starts with `#!`, and no file that is not Markdown, text, JSON, YAML or an image (the LICENSE aside); the folder has a README of at least 40 words, an icon and the LICENSE, and neither marketplace file lists it. | `tests/test_plugins.py::SkillBundleTest::test_the_bundle_holds_only_what_the_directory_takes` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-019 | the skill of plugins/wingbox-skill must be byte for byte SKILL.md and references/ of the repository and its LICENSE the repository's; after SKILL.md or the release changes, `tools/sync_plugin.py --check` must exit 1 and name the stale files of both plugin folders, and one run of `tools/sync_plugin.py` must bring both to the same version and skill. | `tests/test_plugins.py::SkillBundleTest::test_one_sync_keeps_both_folders_current` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-020 | a file of references/ that the plugin directory refuses (a script, any file that starts with `#!`, a suffix outside Markdown, text, JSON, YAML and images) must be copied into the full plugin and left out of plugins/wingbox-skill by `tools/sync_plugin.py`, and `--check` must pass with it left out. | `tests/test_plugins.py::SkillBundleTest::test_the_bundle_leaves_out_what_the_directory_refuses` |
| REQ-PLUGIN-021 | SKILL.md must say what to do when the `wingbox` program is missing and the skill has no scripts (a skill installed from a plugin directory): tell the user, install it with `pipx install wingbox` or `uv tool install wingbox`, naming the package only (no index address, no git source), then build the graph with `wingbox build .`, which writes only `.wingbox/`, and leave `wingbox install` to the user. | `tests/test_plugins.py::SkillBundleTest::test_the_skill_says_what_to_do_without_the_program` |

## PORT

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-PORT-001 | portable.lock takes the exclusive lock of a file (a second handle cannot take it without waiting) and portable.unlock releases it, on every system. | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_a_lock_is_exclusive_and_released` |
| REQ-PORT-002 | portable.replace moves a file over an existing one. | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_replace_writes_the_new_file` |
| REQ-PORT-003 | a build whose worker processes are spawned (the only method on Windows) publishes the same graph, byte for byte, as a build with the default start method; enough modules to use the parallel resolution. | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_the_graph_is_the_same_bytes_whatever_the_start_method` |
| REQ-PORT-004 | source files with CRLF line endings (as Windows editors and Git checkouts write them) give the same graph, line numbers included, as the same files with LF; the graph file itself is written with LF on every system. | `tests/test_portable.py::LineEndingTest::test_crlf_sources_give_the_same_graph_as_lf_sources` |
| REQ-PORT-005 | a path given by a person or a hook is normalised at the edge by one function: on Windows backslashes, forward slashes, a Git Bash drive (/c/Users/me), a lower-case drive letter and the \\\\?\\ long-path prefix all become the same C:\\Users\\me form; on macOS and Linux only the redundant parts (a//b, ./a) go. | `tests/test_portable.py::EdgePathTest::test_paths_from_users_and_hooks_take_the_systems_form` |
| REQ-PORT-006 | relative_posix gives a path under the root with / on every system, "" for the root itself, and None for a path outside it (a parent, a sibling whose name starts like the root, another drive); no string prefix test decides it. | `tests/test_portable.py::EdgePathTest::test_a_path_is_relative_to_the_root_or_outside_it` |
| REQ-PORT-010 | every source file compiles with warnings turned into errors, so a user never sees a SyntaxWarning (an invalid escape sequence, for example) the first time Wingbox runs on a new Python version. | `tests/test_no_syntax_warnings.py::NoSyntaxWarningsTest::test_sources_compile_without_warnings` |
| REQ-PORT-011 | the detached refresh (started with portable.detached_process_options, as the hook starts it), a console child it starts as it is, a child started through portable.hidden_process_options and a multiprocessing spawn worker have no visible console window; with the flags of 0.7.0 to 0.8.1 (DETACHED_PROCESS) the same children get a console window of their own, which is the window the report saw. | `tests/test_windows_console.py::WindowsConsoleTest::test_the_background_refresh_and_its_children_show_no_console_window` |
| REQ-PORT-012 | portable.hidden_process_options gives CREATE_NO_WINDOW and a hidden STARTUPINFO on Windows and nothing elsewhere; portable.detached_process_options never combines CREATE_NO_WINDOW with DETACHED_PROCESS or CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE (Windows then ignores it). | `tests/test_windows_console.py::HiddenOptionsTest::test_the_helpers_add_flags_only_on_windows` |
| REQ-PORT-013 | portable.read_text returns the text of a file, None when it does not exist, and on Windows tries again while the system reports the file in use (another process is replacing it); elsewhere a PermissionError is raised at once. | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_a_text_file_another_process_is_replacing_is_read_after_a_moment` |
| REQ-PORT-014 | prepare_wingbox_directory reads .wingbox/.gitignore through portable.read_text, so a second process that starts while the first is replacing the file (two MCP servers started together on Windows) waits a moment and goes on. | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_two_processes_preparing_the_folder_do_not_stop_on_its_gitignore` |
| REQ-PORT-015 | portable.worker_count is WINGBOX_JOBS when that is a whole number of at least 1, every processor otherwise (unset, empty, zero, negative, not a number), and never more than the number of tasks | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_the_worker_count_follows_the_jobs_setting` |
| REQ-PORT-016 | with WINGBOX_JOBS=1 the builder parses its files without starting a pool of worker processes | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_one_job_parses_in_this_process` |
| REQ-PORT-017 | the processor count is read in one place, portable.worker_count, so WINGBOX_JOBS limits every pool: the build, the incremental build, the use positions and the background refresh | `tests/test_portable.py::PortableTest::test_no_code_reads_the_processor_count_but_the_helper` |

## PY

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-PY-001 | public module-level names bound by assignment (plain, annotated, Final, TypeAlias, tuple targets) or listed in \_\_all\_\_ are entities of kind variable; private names and bare annotations are not. | `tests/test_symbol_usages.py::PythonSymbolUsageTests::test_module_level_variables_are_entities` |
| REQ-PY-002 | a `type X = ...` alias (Python 3.12) is an entity like an assignment. | `tests/test_symbol_usages.py::PythonSymbolUsageTests::test_type_statement_is_an_entity` |
| REQ-PY-003 | `from m import X`, `from pkg import m` + `m.X` and `import pkg.m as mm` + `mm.X` reference X of m, for variables, classes and functions. | `tests/test_symbol_usages.py::PythonSymbolUsageTests::test_imported_and_qualified_variables_are_referenced` |
| REQ-PY-004 | a name re-exported by a package \_\_init\_\_.py (from .shared import X) is referenced at its defining file by the re-exporting file and by every importer of the package; module dependencies (impact) do not change. | `tests/test_symbol_usages.py::PythonSymbolUsageTests::test_reexports_through_init_lead_to_the_definition` |
| REQ-PY-005 | two modules defining the same name are told apart by the import, and a parameter or local variable that shadows an imported module is not a use of the module's names. | `tests/test_symbol_usages.py::PythonSymbolUsageTests::test_name_collisions_and_shadowing_name_no_other_file` |
| REQ-PY-006 | a Python file saved with a UTF-8 byte order mark (Visual Studio and some Windows editors write one) must be parsed like any other: its functions and classes are in the graph, a file that imports it gets its reference edges, and the build prints no "AST parse failed" warning for it. | `tests/test_issue_105_bom.py::ByteOrderMarkTest::test_a_python_file_with_a_bom_keeps_its_functions_and_classes` |
| REQ-PY-007 | a Python file whose comment or string names a validator, that declares only a shape (TypedDict, NamedTuple, a typed dataclass, a frozen dataclass, a dataclass with \_\_post_init\_\_), or that imports a validation library without using it is not a validation module: no evidence, and an import of it gives no data-flow fact. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::PythonValidationTest::test_text_that_only_names_a_validator_gives_no_mark` |
| REQ-PY-008 | a Python file is a validation module when its syntax tree holds a class whose base is a pydantic model class, a pydantic validator decorator, a pydantic schema call, or an attrs field with a validator, each named through an import of the library; the evidence is the kind and the qualified name. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::PythonValidationTest::test_a_validator_defined_in_the_file_is_the_evidence` |
| REQ-PY-009 | a base, decorator or call counts only when its name is bound by the import of the validation library: a class of the file named BaseModel, a name of another module, a name that a parameter, a later import or a later glob import also binds, and an attrs class or field without a validator give no evidence. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::PythonValidationTest::test_a_name_that_is_not_the_imported_validator_gives_nothing` |
| REQ-PY-010 | an import of a validation module is written as (data-flow &lt;src> &lt;tgt> :validation-module :evidence "&lt;kind> &lt;name>"); the retired mark :validated is never written, and a parse result that claims validation without evidence gives no fact. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::PythonValidationTest::test_the_code_graph_writes_the_mark_with_its_evidence` |
| REQ-PY-011 | the parse cache stores the evidence with the result and reads it back; its schema version is above 5, so a result cached with the flag of the text detector is parsed again. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::PythonValidationTest::test_the_parse_cache_keeps_the_evidence_and_drops_older_results` |
| REQ-PY-012 | in a project that keeps its package under `src/`, a file outside it that imports the package by name (`from shop.pricing import total`, `from shop import checkout`, `import shop.pricing`) depends on the module under `src/`: `rdeps` and `impact` of the module name the tests, and `usages` of a function lists its uses in them. | `tests/test_python_src_layout.py::SourceFolderTest::test_a_test_reaches_the_package_under_src` |
| REQ-PY-013 | when several parts of a repository each keep a package of the same name under their own `src/`, a test reaches the `src/` nearest to it, never the one of another part. | `tests/test_python_src_layout.py::SourceFolderTest::test_each_part_of_a_monorepo_reaches_its_own_src` |
| REQ-PY-014 | a package at the root of the repository is still what `from shop...` means, also when a folder `src/` holds a package of the same name; and a name that no folder of the repository holds is no dependency. | `tests/test_python_src_layout.py::SourceFolderTest::test_a_package_beside_the_tests_still_wins` |
| REQ-PY-015 | in a generated project whose tests import every class through a facade of two levels of `from .x import *`, each test resolves its classes to the files that define them, and with four times the models no file under .wingbox/ (graph, use file, cache, fragment store, snapshot, ...) is more than about four times as large. | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::GrowthTest::test_the_facade_is_followed_and_every_file_of_wingbox_grows_with_the_project` |

| REQ-PY-018 | for every Python file of this repository and a file with every binding form, the index of the one walk gives the nodes of any classes below any function, class or loop (and below the module) as the same objects in the same order as `ast.walk` of that node. | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::OneWalkTest::test_the_index_gives_the_nodes_in_the_order_of_a_walk` |
| REQ-PY-019 | for every function and class of every Python file of this repository and of a file with every binding form (assignments, imports, `as` names, pattern captures, nested definitions, `global`, walrus in a comprehension, a decorator or a default), the names its body binds read from the index are the names the definition gives (a walk of the body that stops at nested scopes). | `tests/test_issue_157_build_growth.py::OneWalkTest::test_the_names_a_scope_binds_are_the_same_from_the_index` |

| REQ-PY-022 | `import depot` and `from depot import ledger` mean the package folder `depot/` when a module file `depot.py` lies beside it, as for Python's own finder: the importing file, `depot.py` included, depends on `depot/__init__.py` and nothing depends on `depot.py`. | `tests/test_python_package_and_module.py::PackageBesideModuleTest::test_the_name_means_the_package_folder` |
| REQ-PY-023 | `from . import crate` in a folder `depot/` that has no `__init__.py` depends on `depot/crate` only, also when a module file `depot.py` lies beside the folder; `import depot` then still means `depot.py`. | `tests/test_python_package_and_module.py::PackageBesideModuleTest::test_a_folder_without_init_is_not_the_module_beside_it` |

| REQ-PY-026 | an absolute import that two source roots hold is linked to neither; the `src/` nearest to the importing file still wins before the source roots are asked; a name of the standard library is never looked for in a source root; a folder a manifest names outside the repository is not read; and a manifest that cannot be read is named in a warning and leaves the build whole. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::PythonSourceRootTests::test_a_name_that_several_source_roots_hold_stays_unlinked` |

| REQ-PY-028 | inside a package (the folder of the importing file holds `__init__.py`) `import logging` is not the sibling `logging.py` or the sibling folder `queue/`: Python 3 looks an absolute name up on the import path, where the folder of a package is not; in a folder that is not a package (a script folder) the sibling is still what the name means. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::PythonImportFormTests::test_an_absolute_import_inside_a_package_is_not_the_sibling_module` |
| REQ-PY-029 | `usages` lists the uses of a name that an import inside a function or a class body binds, as it lists those behind an import at the top of the file: `hook.f()` after `from pkg import hook`, `f()` after `from pkg.hook import f`, `pkg.hook.f()` after `import pkg.hook`, `h.f()` after `import pkg.hook as h`, the constructor call of an imported class and a method called on what it builds; a parameter of the same name still shadows the import of another function, and `entity` names the file for a chain that only such an import reaches. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::ScopeImportTests::test_a_name_imported_inside_a_function_or_a_class_is_followed` |
| REQ-PY-030 | `usages <file>::Class.method` lists a call through the class name (`Store.create()`, `store.Store.create()`), a method passed as a value (`Store.create`) and a call on a module-level instance (`db = Store()` or `db: Store = make()`, then `db.query()`), in the defining file and in the files that import the names; the class keeps its own reference at each of those places, and a variable bound in two ways has no type. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::MemberUseTests::test_python_class_and_instance_members` |
| REQ-PY-031 | a Python file with an expression of 3,000 terms, a chain of 3,000 calls, 3,000 `elif` branches, a module name joined from 3,000 strings (a name longer than a file name may be) or a decorator called 3,000 times does not stop the build: its imports, the class defined after the long part (with its method and its base) and the use of a name in that class are read as in any file. Where Python's own parser refuses the file (3.11, beyond about 1,500 chained calls), the build warns, goes on and keeps the imports of the file. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::LongTreeTests::test_python_reads_a_file_with_long_chains` |
| REQ-PY-032 | for a Python file that `ast.parse` refuses, every module of `import a.b as x, c.d` and every name of `from pkg import target, other`, also over the lines of a list in parentheses and after a `;`, is read: the file depends on each module and submodule named; a name inside a string of the file is no import. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::TextReaderTests::test_a_python_file_the_parser_refuses_keeps_every_import` |
| REQ-PY-033 | the classes with an abstract base (`ABC`, `ABCMeta` or `Protocol` among the bases or the keywords of the class statement, also written over several lines) are read from the syntax tree, and from the class lines of a file the parser refuses: a comment or a string that repeats `class A(ABC ` for 100 KB on one line is read in well under two minutes, where the expression used before took hours, and names no class. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::TextReaderTests::test_abstract_classes_are_read_from_the_tree_in_linear_time` |
| REQ-PY-034 | the bound of a type parameter of a class or a function (`class Box[T: Store]`, `def keep[T: Store](x: T)`, Python 3.12) is a use of that name as a type. | `tests/test_review_wp5_py_ts_rust.py::ScopeImportTests::test_the_bound_of_a_type_parameter_is_a_use` |

| REQ-PY-036 | a module name built from a literal package path and one variable (f-string or +) is a registry wire to every module of that package named by a string value of a JSON, YAML or TOML file in the folder of the loading file; values that name no module of that package give nothing. | `tests/test_python_dynamic_loading.py::DynamicLoadingTest::test_a_module_name_filled_from_a_configuration_file_is_a_registry_wire` |
| REQ-PY-037 | a module name that is only a variable, that has more than one variable, or whose values are listed only in a configuration file of another folder gives no edge. | `tests/test_python_dynamic_loading.py::DynamicLoadingTest::test_a_name_without_a_literal_package_or_a_configuration_beside_the_loader_gives_no_wire` |
| REQ-PY-038 | in a loop, a method called on an object that a registry module returns for the loop variable is a call into each module the registry wires that defines the method; when the call result replaces one of its arguments (x = obj.m(x, ...)), the modules of one configuration list pass the data on in the order of that list. | `tests/test_python_dynamic_loading.py::DynamicLoadingTest::test_a_method_called_on_an_object_looked_up_in_a_registry_calls_the_wired_modules` |
| REQ-PY-039 | the code graph writes (registry-wires &lt;loader> &lt;module> :config &lt;file>), registry calls as (calls ... :registry) and pipeline order as (data-flow ... :pipeline); `impact` of a wired module names the loader and the caller, and a pipeline step does not make the previous step its dependent. | `tests/test_python_dynamic_loading.py::DynamicLoadingTest::test_the_code_graph_writes_the_new_facts_and_impact_follows_them` |

## REL

| Requirement | Behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-REL-001 | every `uses:` of .github/workflows/\*.yml that is not a local `./` action names its action at a full commit (40 hexadecimal characters after the @) and carries the release tag of that commit as a trailing comment (`# vX.Y.Z`): a tag can be moved to other code, a commit cannot. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowPinTest::test_every_action_names_a_full_commit` |
| REQ-REL-002 | `tools/release_files.py <dist>` writes &lt;dist>/SHA256SUMS with one line per file of the folder, the SBOM it writes included, SHA256SUMS itself and files whose name starts with a dot (the .gitignore `uv build` writes there) not: the SHA-256 in hexadecimal, two spaces, the file name, sorted by name, LF line ends (the form `shasum -a 256 -c` and `sha256sum -c` read); a second run writes the same bytes. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::ReleaseFilesTest::test_checksum_file_lists_every_file_of_the_folder` |
| REQ-REL-003 | `tools/release_files.py <dist>` writes &lt;dist>/&lt;name>-&lt;version>.cdx.json, a CycloneDX 1.5 JSON document: the package of pyproject.toml as metadata.component (name, version, purl `pkg:pypi/<name>@<version>`, its license expression) with one file component per built file of that distribution in the folder (name and SHA-256; a file of another distribution is not one of them); one library component per dependency with its name as written, its pinned version and its purl with the normalized name, an environment marker kept as a property, a dependency named only by an extra marked optional and one named twice listed once; the dependency graph from the package to each of them; no timestamp and a serial number derived from the content, so that a second run writes the same bytes. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::ReleaseFilesTest::test_sbom_lists_the_package_its_files_and_its_pinned_dependencies` |
| REQ-REL-004 | `tools/release_files.py` exits 1, names the cause on standard error and writes neither file when a dependency of pyproject.toml is not pinned with `==` (an SBOM names versions, not ranges), when the folder holds no built file of the package at its version, and when the folder does not exist. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::ReleaseFilesTest::test_a_dependency_without_an_exact_version_or_a_folder_without_the_package_is_refused` |
| REQ-REL-005 | for the pyproject.toml of this repository the SBOM names the package at its version and exactly the distributions that `dependencies` and the extras name, each once with the version pinned there; the tool imports the standard library only. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::ReleaseFilesTest::test_the_sbom_of_this_repository_names_every_dependency_of_the_package` |
| REQ-REL-006 | `uv build`, the build command of a release, run twice on the same tree into two folders with the same SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH writes a source archive and a wheel that are byte for byte the same in both folders, and leaves no file behind in the tree; skipped, and reported as skipped, when uv is not on PATH. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::ReproducibleBuildTest::test_two_builds_of_the_same_tree_give_the_same_bytes` |
| REQ-REL-007 | the check of REQ-REL-001 reads a workflow as YAML, so it also names an action at a tag that is written with a comment of several words, in flow style (`{uses: ...}`) or under a quoted key, and an action at a commit whose comment is not the release tag alone; an action at a commit with its tag and a local `./` action pass. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowPinTest::test_no_way_of_writing_an_action_escapes_the_pin_check` |
| REQ-REL-008 | every `run` step of a workflow that pipes one command into another (`docker run ... \| tee`, `wingbox build ... \| tail`) fails when the first command fails: it runs under `shell: bash`, which sets pipefail, or its first command is `set -o pipefail` (`set -euo pipefail`); without it GitHub's default `bash -e` takes the status of the last command. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_a_pipeline_fails_when_its_first_command_fails` |
| REQ-REL-009 | in map-request.yml, which builds a repository named in an issue anyone can open, no secret is in the environment of the workflow or of the job: a step holds the token exactly when its script calls `gh`, so the steps that clone, install, build and collect run without it. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_the_map_request_gives_the_token_only_to_the_steps_that_call_gh` |
| REQ-REL-010 | map-request.yml removes `target/.wingbox` right after the clone and before Wingbox is installed or run, so a report, a map or a configuration that the requested repository commits is never taken for what the build wrote. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_the_map_request_removes_a_committed_wingbox_folder_before_the_build` |
| REQ-REL-011 | the build step of map-request.yml, run as GitHub runs it, exits non-zero when `wingbox build` fails or is stopped by its time limit, although its output goes through `tail`; it exits 0 when the build succeeds. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_a_failed_build_of_the_map_request_fails_the_step` |
| REQ-REL-012 | the collect step of map-request.yml copies REPORT.md and map.html into the uploaded folder only when each is a regular file in a real `target/.wingbox` folder: when one of them, or the folder, is a symbolic link (to a file of the runner) or is missing, the step fails and the uploaded folder holds no content of the linked file. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_the_map_request_uploads_only_regular_files_the_build_wrote` |
| REQ-REL-013 | the job of publish-mcp.yml that waits for the version on PyPI has a time limit longer than the longest its wait loop can take (every attempt: the time limit of its request plus its pause), so a version that never appears ends with the error of the loop and not with a cancelled job; the request of every attempt has a time limit. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_the_registry_job_outlives_its_own_wait` |
| REQ-REL-014 | tests.yml and docs.yml run on a push to `main` only (a pull request runs them for every other branch): their lists of branches name no feature branch, which would stay there after the branch is gone. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_the_push_triggers_name_no_branch_but_main` |
| REQ-REL-015 | docs.yml, the only workflow a change of Markdown files starts, runs the test modules that compare a document with the code or with result files: tests.test_english_only, test_readme_numbers, test_plugins, test_mcpb, test_assurance_docs, test_output_formats_cli (SKILL.md, REQ-INSTALL-045), test_json_errors (references/code-mode.md, REQ-INSTALL-046), test_mcp_registry (README, REQ-MCP-014), test_benchmark_report and test_benchmark_cpp_collisions (the benchmark READMEs, REQ-BENCH-001, -008, -009, -011, -024) and test_review_wp9_mcp_ci_docs (SKILL.md, the README, AGENTS.md, ROADMAP.md, the limits page); every module it names exists. | `tests/test_release_integrity.py::WorkflowSafetyTest::test_a_change_of_documents_runs_every_test_that_reads_a_document` |

| REQ-RUBY-009 | Rails through/source associations resolve their declared model while polymorphic associations remain unresolved. | `tests/test_ruby_language.py::RubyLanguageTests::test_association_source_resolves_and_polymorphic_target_stays_open` |
| REQ-RUBY-010 | Function search returns the precise Ruby method entity once when a class also lists that method. | `tests/test_code_graph_search.py::CodeGraphSearchAliasTest::test_qualified_ruby_method_is_not_duplicated_by_class_summary` |
| REQ-RUBY-011 | a Ruby file is a validation module when the body of a class or module, or the `included do` block of a concern, calls validates, validate or a validates\_... method without a receiver; the evidence names the method and the class. A comment, a string, a call inside a method or a call on a receiver gives no evidence. | `tests/test_validation_mark.py::OtherLanguagesValidationTest::test_ruby` |
| REQ-RUBY-012 | A Ruby file with a sum of 3,000 operands is parsed: its class, its constant and its dependencies are those of the file. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::DeepTreeTest::test_a_ruby_expression_of_three_thousand_operands_is_read` |
| REQ-RUBY-013 | `has_many` of a plural in -ches, -shes, -xes, -sses, -uses, -ies and of an irregular plural (people, children, analyses) is an association with the model of the singular name. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::RubyRailsNamesTest::test_has_many_names_are_singularized_as_rails_does` |
| REQ-RUBY-014 | The singular of a Rails association name follows the rules of Rails; a word that is not a plural is left as it is. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::RubyRailsNamesTest::test_the_singular_rules` |
| REQ-RUBY-015 | `User::LIMIT`, with LIMIT defined in a concern that User includes, is a dependency on the file of `User`: a scoped name that resolves nowhere is resolved by its longest leading part; a name two files define stays unlinked. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::RubyRailsNamesTest::test_a_scoped_constant_names_at_least_its_scope` |
| REQ-RUBY-016 | The autoload paths of config/application.rb are read in linear time: 100 KB of one line of `autoload_paths` without a path, or of unclosed `Rails.root.join(`, within a second; the paths of a real line are still found. | `tests/test_review_wp6_other_langs.py::RubyRailsNamesTest::test_the_autoload_path_scan_is_linear` |
